Jessa Duggar Praises 'Angel' Mom Michelle Duggar's Role as Grandparent

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Pregnant Jessa Duggar is happy to give mom Michelle Duggar her flowers as she prepares to welcome baby No. 7.

“Every time that we’ve had a baby, she’s there at the hospital helping us through,” Jessa, 33, said of her mother, 60, during the Wednesday, October 28, episode of the “Unplanned Podcast” hosted by Matt and Abby Howard. “She’s just a calming presence. She’s an angel.”

Michelle, who has 45 grandchildren and counting, has been “the hugest blessing” for Jessa as she raises her six kids alongside husband Ben Seewald.

According to Jessa, her mom is the key to her postpartum recovery after delivering each and every baby.

“My postpartum pregnancy and labor secret is — my mom is an angel,” she shared, calling both Michelle and her dad, Jim Bob Duggar, “the best.”

Jessa said her mom’s help specifically, whether it’s staying up all night with her newborns or cooking and cleaning in those early days, contributes “so much to just how easy it is to recover.”

The Counting On alum revealed that Michelle is also a great help at the hospital.

“She was like, ‘You do not look well. You go home, get a shower, get some sleep in your own bed. I’m here. I’ve got this,'” Jessa said, recalling a moment during her sixth pregnancy when her husband, 31, clearly needed a break. “I was like, ‘Go, babe!’ I know my mom’s got this.”

Once she’s home from the hospital, Jessa said her mom is a go-to babysitter for their brood, who they call “Lolli.” The little ones are especially pleased when Lolli shows up, because she always brings things with her to entertain them.

“She calls it her Lolli bag,” Seewald explained to the podcast listeners, describing his mother-in-law’s purse. “And they’re just like, ‘Lolli, did you bring your Lolli bag?!'”

According to Seewald, Michelle, the mother of 19 children, takes pride in putting her loved ones first.

“She gets so much joy out of just helping others,” he shared. “It just brings so much brightness to her day just to be able to make someone else’s life a little bit better.”

Like her mother, Jessa is a fan of big families and bringing more love into the world.

She announced last month that she and Seewald are expecting their seventh baby. The couple, who tied the knot in 2014, already share sons Spurgeon, 10, Henry, 9, George, 2, and Eddie, 15 months, and daughters Ivy, 7, and Fern, 5.

“It’s always me,” Jessa said on the podcast when asked whose idea it was to try for another baby. “I get baby fever way too soon. I’m like, ‘How can I have a newborn and have baby fever?’ When I said, ‘We should have a No. 7,’ he was like, ‘Alright.’”

Seewald added, “She’s the one carrying this child. … If she wants to have another one, wants to be a mom again, who am I to stand in the way of that?”

The biggest modding site in PC gaming is taking a stance on AI

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Posts from this topic will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Posts from this topic will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Posts from this topic will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Nexus Mods says that authors of newly uploaded mods will have to declare whether AI was ‘used in their work.’

Nexus Mods says that authors of newly uploaded mods will have to declare whether AI was ‘used in their work.’

Posts from this author will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Posts from this author will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

The PC gaming mods repository Nexus Mods is making disclosures about AI mandatory for uploads of new mods, meaning that mod authors will have to declare “whether AI has been used in their work,” the site announced today as part of a big post about AI-generated content on the platform.

“One of the most consistent concerns we’ve heard is that it’s not clearly disclosed when AI has been used to create a mod,” according to the post from site owner Victor “Foledinho” Folmann. “We already have tags for AI-generated and AI-assisted mods, but we recognise that there’s a lot more we can do to make that information more accessible and clear for you all.” Any mods tagged as “AI-assisted” will also be required to include “a public explanation of how and where AI was used.” AI tags will also be “more prominent” on mod pages.

Folmann says Nexus Mods is “pro-human creativity” but “won’t introduce an outright ban on AI-generated mods.” As for why that is:

The answer is simple: AI is currently used throughout the whole process of creation, from script autocompletion to generation of textures, voices, and full assets, and it is difficult to draw a clear boundary between what is acceptable and unacceptable. An outright ban will not eliminate AI from the modding process but will prevent people from mentioning its use. We’d prefer the opposite; it should be out in the open, clearly labelled, and regulated according to the rules that we can implement on our platform.

Folmann notes that Nexus Mods won’t use mods to train AI and “will not sell or license them to anyone doing so.” However, the site is working on “clearer control” over how mods can be reused on others and strengthening attribution for when “a mod uses another Mod Author’s mod with the appropriate permissions.”

Nexus Mods is also making changes to the Recently Added section of the site to address a problem of mod authors uploading many mods at once, which makes other mods fall down down the page. “This is less of an issue related to AI, but it’s becoming much more prevalent as mod production is speeding up,” Folmann says. The changes include trying limits on how many mods from one author are shown at a time and “tighter restrictions” on uploads from new accounts. The platform is also launching controls to hide AI-generated content in game pages and on the homepage, and “next up,” in search.

Nexus Mods’ full AI policy is here, and mod authors have 60 days to update their existing mods to meet the requirements. The 60-day grace period ends on January 2nd, and after that time, the site will begin enforcing the rules.

Carrick: “Roma are a great team, it was always going to be difficult.”

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Following United’s 3-2 comeback win, Red Devils manager Michael Carrick spoke at a press conference analyzing the match against Roma.

“I’m really proud of my players; they deserve great respect for being able to turn the game around. The fans also did their part; they wanted to inspire us, and their support was palpable.”

“After the first half, there was a lot of frustration, but in the locker room, I reminded the boys how important the fans were. In the second half, we maintained a positive attitude and continued to believe until the end. It’s still a victory, nothing more.”

“It’s certainly a very significant result. Some players experienced sensations they hadn’t yet experienced at Old Trafford, and for the new arrivals, it was crucial to feel the support of the fans. Sometimes, it’s not easy to figure out how to overcome difficulties. Now, however, we must stay calm and focus on the next match.”

“We weren’t in an easy situation. Roma are a top-notch team, they marked the entire pitch individually, and we knew it would be a tough match. We left our opponents too much space, but gradually we managed to get closer to their goal. We knew they could cause us problems, but in those moments you have to keep believing in what you’re doing, without giving in to frustration.”

“In the first half, we could have lost confidence, especially after what happened to Bruno Fernandes on the penalty and Baleba’s exit. Tielemans made a good impact coming off the bench. We wanted to give this victory to our fans.”

China Truck Sales Now Majority Electric. What’s Next?

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

EV heavy trucks hit 50.3% market share in China for September, with sales up 34% from a year ago. Long-haul big rig market share hit 64.5%. China is on track to have over 40% EV market share for the entire year, reaching their 2030 goal in a matter of months. This is not the first time China’s heavy truck market share topped 50%, but that previous blip in December last year was prior to subsidies ending, followed by a dip in January. Current sales rates are not bolstered by the subsidy and are now on a much more stable upward trajectory.

The speed of EV adoption for heavy trucks in China has recently grown much faster than for cars, having risen from just 13.6% in 2024. When I visited Guangzhou in November of last year and again in April of this year, I noticed a lot more green plates on trucks, indicating that they were electric. It seems that my perception is backed by sales data.

With the addition of LNG trucks, diesel’s share of heavy trucks has dropped to roughly 40%. While LNG sales have also declined with the rate of EV adoption, diesel trucks could soon make up less than 1/3 of sales and decline further from there. China could feasibly double its 2030 goal and hit 80% EV penetration, by which point many diesel models are likely to be discontinued.

With these levels of market penetration, in addition to retirements, China’s diesel demand is on track to fall by about 230,000 barrels per day this year, despite increased freight movement. Every additional electric truck that replaces a diesel truck then builds on that reduction. Due to their duty cycle, each electric truck sold has a much greater impact on fuel consumption than each car sold. And the price of fuel combined with the duty cycle has a much greater impact on overall truck economics.

To be clear, these improving results are not due to subsidies, as they appear in the year after subsidies were cut. They are also not due to mandates, as the 2030 goals were met before the ink was dry on their announcement. EV trucks are now more affordable to own and operate than diesel trucks in China. The increase in diesel prices has only increased the cost gap.

Last year, roughly 90% of all electric truck sales globally were in China. That percentage is set to expand this year, despite rising sales in some other countries. Those sales are simply not rising fast enough outside of China to keep pace with China. In comparison, the EU is looking at 2.5% EV market share this year. China is on pace to average roughly as many electric trucks sold per week as Europe sells per year. The US EV truck market looks like a rounding error in comparison at less than one percent.

In addition, China has rapidly been building out heavy truck charging infrastructure as part of the 28 million charging facilities planned to be in operation by the end of 2027, up from 21 million last March. This not only focuses on the major cities but also extends into more rural areas that have been slower to adopt electrification. In addition, several companies are building out battery swap stations. While we have yet to see if battery swap or MW+ charging wins in the truck market, expect the competition between the two approaches to be fierce.

Within Tier 1 cities, I could see a potential phase out of diesel trucks in the near future. Hainan province is already planning to end ICE passenger car sales by 2030, and I expect that to extend to diesel trucks as well. Although rural areas have been slower to adopt, they also tend to be less wealthy and thus more impacted by the changing economics. If diesel prices stay elevated while charging becomes convenient, many rural businesses will not be able to afford to buy new diesel trucks. With the end of diesel truck sales, strict emissions standards and a potential return of scrappage incentives could take remaining diesel vehicles off the road at an accelerated rate.

With fewer diesel trucks on the road in the largest market globally, diesel consumption is set to decline. In addition, the supply chain for diesel parts will also start to decline. As protectionism has targeted electric vehicles, rather than internal combustion  engine (ICE) vehicles, a decline in Chinese (ICE) parts has a potentially significant impact on global markets.

Of course, as EVs take over the majority of heavy truck sales in the fiercely competitive Chinese market, Chinese manufacturers will start looking outside of the country for growth.

The traditional thought would be to look at wealthy Western countries that have substantial buying power. However, many Chinese companies may be a bit reluctant to focus on those markets after the protectionist efforts against electric passenger vehicles. Many European markets, like Germany, also have very high electricity prices, which makes the financial proposition less attractive for commercial vehicles that are heavily motivated by costs. Chinese companies may start focusing elsewhere.

The Middle Corridor could become a focus. This essentially rebuilds the Silk Road land route from China, across the Caspian Sea, on to Turkey. Many of the countries along this route  once had prosperous trade, only to fall into decline when ocean trade routes were established. However, land transport is much easier to electrify than long ocean voyages. China is investing heavily with countries like Kazakhstan along the route with renewable electricity infrastructure. Electrified rail is expanding by 26-fold in Turkey. Electrified trucking is the next logical step, connecting the rail lines to more local production along the route.

Other Belt and Road Initiative (BRI) countries could also benefit. In countries like Ethiopia, which has expensive diesel but electricity prices to businesses of just $0.017/kWh, electric trucks could make perfect sense to transport between new factories and new ports. Trading partners like Vietnam and Thailand could also see an influx.

All this is not to say that local companies will not benefit. Unlike passenger cars, heavy equipment tends to be more modular. Due to the bulk and mass of the vehicles, final assembly is more likely to be localized, with greater use of local suppliers.

Overall, we could see much of the “Rest of the World” (ROW) leapfrog legacy economies in EV truck adoption. The purchase price is no longer prohibitive in China, and the operating costs are much lower. With infrastructure buildout and increasing renewable installed capacity, electric trucking is poised to take over rapidly.

That is not to say that the West will be completely left out. Countries with less prohibitive trade policies toward EVs will likely see increased adoption. While EVs have taken over passenger car sales in Norway, their trucking industry still has a way to go at just 14.4%. And the geographically most eastern country typically lumped in with the “West,” Australia, is likely to see EV truck sales follow a similar pattern to EV car sales. If Canada continues to open up, they could also benefit. Of course, as the majority of the economic benefit from electric trucks comes from their use, inflated trucking costs hurt every company that moves products by truck, and those inflated costs then lead to inflation throughout the economy. That impacts overall affordability. As such, there is a chance that protectionists do not put as much effort into undermining electric truck imports as they put into passenger EVs.

And the EV heavy truck global supply chain will become increasingly well established. This could mean more parts availability for brands outside of China to build competitive vehicles, if those companies have access to global supply chains. Competitive markets will also drive innovation, moving the technology forward at an accelerating rate. Meanwhile the supply chain for diesel trucks will shrink.

While the US still seems to be figuring out how to make a safe EV Semi, battery-powered heavy trucks are becoming increasingly mature products elsewhere. As electric trucks move from majority share to complete market dominance in the largest market, other markets will follow, and the transition will become inevitable. Hopefully, the largest oil producing country in the world will not be left too far behind.

Larry lives in Queens, NY, with his wife and cats. While he has spent much of his professional career leading global marketing efforts, his passions focus on clean technology and the automotive industry. He believes in Green Free Trade and the potential for the application of engineering talent to create solutions to the world’s most challenging problems.

Peter Schmeichel names Manchester United star who ‘changed the game’ against Roma

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Peter Schmeichel praised Manchester United summer signing Youri Tielemans for ‘changing the game’ in the Champions League comeback win over Roma.

United’s worst start to a season since the 1980s looked set to take another blow as Roma stormed into a 2-0 lead at Old Trafford.

Matias Soule took advantage of an alarming amount of room near the United box to put the Italians ahead inside 12 minutes.

Manchester United looked on course for a fourth straight defeat when Donyell Malen doubled Rome’s lead from the penalty spot after Ayden Heaven blocked a shot with his arm.

Sign up to our daily football newsletter, pick your team and get the latest news sent directly to your inbox every morning.

The home side were booed off at half-time but improved after the break and got a lifeline when Bruno Fernandes scored just before the hour mark from a retaken penalty.

United were still heading for another defeat and Michael Carrick for the sack until Matheus Cunha scored twice late on to complete a memorable European comeback.

Manchester United legend Schmeichel says he felt ‘so sad’ at half-time but believes the game changed upon the introduction of summer signing Tielemans.

To view this video please enable JavaScript, and consider upgrading to a web browser that supports HTML5 video

The Belgian midfielder replaced the injured Carlos Baleba shortly after half-time and proceeded to ‘change the game’, according to Schmeichel.

‘It’s about time we did something like this,’ Schmeichel told CBS Sports. ‘Manchester United did that type of thing time and time again in the past but it’s been so long.

Use AI to go deeper into the stories you care about – powered by Metro and trusted publications.

‘At half-time I was so sad about what I had seen in that first 45 minutes because it was bad, it was bad. And then Carlos Baleba goes off injured early in the second half and you’re thinking, this can’t get any worse.

MAN UNITED HAVE TURNED IT AROUND!!! 🔴🔥 From 2-0 down, to 3-2 up at Old Trafford 🤩 pic.twitter.com/ATD8wDlc1e

‘But Youri Tielemans came on and he changed the game. That’s the best I’ve seen him play for Man United and I’m so pleased for him.

‘He came into that midfield and he drove the team forward and he was a leader, it was brilliant to see.

‘That for me can be a big turning point. Those players have now felt what it feels like to be a Manchester United player and play in these big European nights.

‘Coming back from 2-0 down, this is what United have been doing for years. I’m so, so happy for Michael Carrick because he needed this win.’

Schmeichel’s son Kasper, the former Leicester City goalkeeper, also singled out Tielemans for praise after the remarkable comeback at Old Trafford.

‘I said at half-time if you’re going to go down, go down fighting,’ he told BBC Radio 5 Live. ‘You can accept losing if you’ve given absolutely everything and they didn’t do that in the first half.

"This place does magical things, even when it doesn't seem like it's going to happen."Michael Carrick tells @Pschmeichel1 how big that win was at Old Trafford for Manchester United 🔥 pic.twitter.com/7ImYOjMoEl

‘But they did in the second half. The tone was set with Carlos Baleba. It was so unfortunate he got an injury for it but that was a spark.

‘Youri Tielemans came on and that changed the game a little bit. There was a little bit more control.

‘When you’re playing in these games and you’re two down, you’re already done, so you may as well go and give it everything you’ve got. You may as well run around.

‘That should be the standard and now the great thing about it for Michael Carrick is now he has something to refer to.

‘Anytime there’s a difficult situation or pre-match team talk, it’s going to be look what you do when you apply yourselves in that manner then you are impossible to live with.’

The comeback win leaves Manchester United 17th in the Champions League after four games, while Roma slip to 19th.

Carrick’s side return to action on Sunday against Aston Villa while Roma, sitting third in Serie A, play host to Sassuolo.

‘We don’t need to save anyone’s job’ – Fernandes defiant after Champions League comeback

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Syndicated reporting provided by Stats Perform and reviewed by the SoccerNews editorial team.

Bruno Fernandes says Manchester United "don't need to save anyone's job" after they ended their nine-game winless streak with a superb comeback victory over Roma in the Champions League.

The pressure has been ramping up on Michael Carrick in recent weeks, with United's last win before Tuesday coming on MD1 of the competition against Sabah in September.

They were, in fact, on their worst winless run since 1988, and things only looked to be getting worse when Matias Soule and Donyell Malen scored to put Roma 2-0 within the first 17 minutes at Old Trafford.

But Fernandes scored a retaken penalty to halve the deficit before Matheus Cunha netted a late brace to complete the comeback, albeit the Brazilian was then sent off for a second bookable offence in the 91st minute.

It has been a tumultuous period for United, with Fernandes playing his part in that after criticising Carrick's tactics following the Red Devils' dismal 4-1 loss to Como.

But the United captain says the club as a whole needed the victory, not just Carrick.

"We don't need to save anyone's job," he told Amazon Prime when asked about Carrick's situation. "The club needed that win, the players needed that win.

"We have been working so hard to get out of this situation together. It's been tough; there's been a lot of speculation in the media.

"A lot of lies around many things that have happened between me and the manager, that we have cleared the air, then after that the manager took the decision to [put me] on the bench because I'm not performing.

"Everyone thought that was a punishment for the words that I had. If it was, it was right. The manager is the one who makes the decisions.

— Manchester United (@ManUtd) November 3, 2026

"It doesn't change the belief I have in the manager and the staff, and every player in this club. It doesn't change my aim to win every game that I have ahead of me.

"It's about performing at this club, getting results and winning games. It's been a long time; we didn't get a win. It's been frustrating for them [the fans] as much as us, too, but we didn't take that the wrong way because it's normal.

"Fans want to win games. They are used to seeing this club at the top, winning trophies, and it's been frustrating to see the club in this situation.

"We are all frustrated with the situation, and we want to get out of this, and hopefully this win gives us the start of something good for us."

Roma became the quickest visiting team to score twice at Old Trafford in major European competition, beating Sporting Braga's record of 20 minutes from October 2012.

However, United were a different side after the break, with 10 of their 15 shots coming in the second half, as well as 2.5 of their 2.7 total expected goals (xG).

Roma, on the other hand, did not manage a single shot in the second half.

Fernandes added: "It's been a tough period for us; we have not been able to play in the way we want, get the results we want.

"We've done some good bits but not had the consistency to do it through a whole game to get results, and it's been tough. But it was important to get a win.

"We knew after that we still had the fans with us; we felt we could change it with a goal and something, and fortunately we did it. Having that belief from their side helps the team a lot, and this was one of those days that Old Trafford came alive."

Gasperini: More lessons for Roma in Man Utd collapse than Real Madrid

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Syndicated reporting provided by Stats Perform and reviewed by the SoccerNews editorial team.

Gian Piero Gasperini insists there are more lessons for Roma to take from their Champions League collapse against Manchester United than from the one against Real Madrid.

Roma looked set to cruise to their first-ever away win in major European competition against United, only to end up on the wrong side of another fightback.

Matias Soule and Donyell Malen had given Roma control just 17 minutes in, with the former profiting from acres of space to slot in before his next shot was blocked by Ayden Heaven's arm, resulting in the spot-kick dispatched by the Dutchman.

Roma had chances to increase their lead, with Soule also hitting the post on the stroke of half-time, but things crumbled after the break.

Gianluca Mancini gave away a penalty that Bruno Fernandes saw saved, but encroachment allowed a re-take, which was converted.

Leonardo Balerdi missed a clearance, which led to United's equaliser from Matheus Cunha, who then fired in the winner in the 86th minute.

Both of Roma's defeats in the competition this season have come after they led by two goals (also 3-4 v Madrid) – they are the first team in European Cup/Champions League history to lose two games from that position in the same season.

"It was a different game to Real Madrid, where it all happened in the last seven or eight minutes from corner kicks, and there were no real signs of the comeback," Gasperini told Sky Sport Italia.

"This time, it was, above all, after the penalty that Manchester United had this sudden surge of enthusiasm, and we were unable to keep up with it.

"I don't think it's a coincidence, but I also think they were different matches. [Against United], we didn't have the strength to press their strikers the way we did in the first half, plus the penalty and the error for the equaliser really turned the tide.

"There is always going to be 15-20 minutes against Manchester United when you're under pressure, and if you can emerge unscathed from it, you'll be fine."

— AS Roma English (@ASRomaEN) November 3, 2026

United were on a nine-game winless streak heading into this clash, with their last victory coming on September 10 against Sabah. The win against Roma is just their third of the campaign in all competitions.

Roma's second-half was atrocious, however. They failed to offer anything going forward, having no shots and no touches in the opposition box.

"Honestly, we learned more from this match than the one with Real Madrid," Gasperini added.

"That felt unpredictable, whereas at times, when Old Trafford was pushing on the team, we need to be able to handle these situations better in future.

"They made modifications at half-time and had nothing left to lose, so they pressed high, so perhaps we should've made more of long balls in that situation.

"The problem was we were no longer able to stick to their reference points, so they had the better on pressing, not letting us play, but also on their attacks. That created some problems for us; we probably should've tried to interrupt their rhythm a bit more with some extra challenges and slow the tempo down.

"The momentum of the match changed with the penalty and the incident for 2-2."

Forgotten Explosives Were Just Discovered at Two National Park Sites

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Glen Canyon Dam rises above the Colorado River near Page, Arizona. The National Park Service temporarily closed nearby Chains Road and The Chains day-use area after workers discovered detonation cord dating to the dam's original construction in the 1950s and 1960s. (Photo: Gabe Ginsberg/Getty)

In a span of days, federal officials had to deal with explosives that were found at National Park Service sites on opposite sides of the country.

On Oct. 30, the National Park Service announced in a public alert that workers had discovered detonation cord near Glen Canyon Dam in Arizona, temporarily closing a recreation area at Lake Powell. The release said the cord was likely left over from the dam’s construction in the fifties or sixties.

That closure came shortly after an Oct. 21 announcement from officials at Maryland’s Antietam National Battlefield, where Marine Corps technicians removed unexploded Civil War-era cannonballs that were embedded in the monument’s concrete base.

While incidents like these are rare, the Park Service has developed procedures for dealing with the discoveries of unexploded munitions or construction explosives. In 2022, the agency began reviewing museum collections for potentially explosive ammunition, according to information officials provided to The Herald-Mail. That same year, NPS published guidance for handling historic munitions, followed by additional safety procedures in 2023 after an investigation involving the transportation of a live 19th-century cannonball from Fort Larned.

Workers found several boreholes containing detonation cord along a restricted service road near Glen Canyon Dam, outside Page, Arizona. The cord is a flexible line filled with high explosives used to initiate blasting charges, according to the National Institute of Standards and Technology.

An explosive ordnance disposal team traced the material to the dam’s original construction in the 1950s and 1960s. Officials said there was no threat to public safety and the dam faced low risk, but closed Chains Road and The Chains day-use area while evaluating mitigation options.

The closure restricts access to a popular shoreline spot for swimming, fishing and picnicking, though the wider national recreation area remains open.

The discovery points back to one of the most ambitious engineering projects in the American West. Construction on the 710-foot-tall Glen Canyon Dam began in 1956, requiring crews to blast diversion tunnels through sandstone canyon walls and excavate foundations down to bedrock.

The project began with a dramatic display of patriotic pyrotechnics on Oct. 15, 1956, when President Dwight D. Eisenhower pressed a telegraph key in Washington, D.C., remotely triggering the first dynamite blast at the site, according to the Bureau of Reclamation.

Nearly 70 years later, the newly discovered detonation cord has brought explosives specialists back to the area, although officials have not linked it to Eisenhower’s ceremonial blast.

Roughly 2,000 miles east, at Antietam National Battlefield in Sharpsburg, Maryland, the Park Service faced historic ammunition of a different sort. On Oct. 21, park officials issued a press release detailing plans to remove ten pieces of cannon ammunition set into the concrete base of the battlefield’s O.T. Reilly Monument. Fought on September 17, 1862, the Battle of Antietam was the bloodiest single-day battle of the American Civil War.

A team of Marine Corps explosive ordnance disposal specialists from Quantico, Virginia, was scheduled to handle the extraction on Oct. 27 and possibly Oct. 28. Because the ammunition might still contain explosives, officials planned to close the visitor center, historic Dunker Church, adjacent roads and two walking trails, keeping the operation closed to public viewing.

The monument’s unusual masonry was the work of Oliver T. Reilly, a local figure who witnessed the Battle of Antietam as a boy in 1862. He spent his adulthood in Sharpsburg, Maryland, working as a battlefield guide, local historian, and collector, according to a historical profile from the National Park Service.

Reilly dedicated his monument in 1927, honoring the Dunker Church, a historic oak tree and local Union Army veterans, the profile said. He embedded 10 artillery projectiles from his private collection into the concrete base, giving a place of worship, an old tree and retired servicemen a rather unexpected amount of firepower.

Antietam isn’t the first park to contend with potentially explosive remnants of its military past. A Civil War-era cannonball interrupted tours at Fort Sumter in 2022, while another discovered at Fort McHenry in 2025 prompted an evacuation before technicians determined it was inert.

At Antietam, officials planned to transport the ammunition to Quantico and return Reilly’s monument to a rebuilt base. At Glen Canyon, no reopening date has been announced for The Chains, leaving a former construction staging ground closed to the people who now come there to swim.

Stratos Data Center Will Emit Air Pollution That’s Linked to Premature Deaths and Health Economic Damages for Utah & States Downwind

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

A new report models emissions from different on-site methane generation power scenarios to show potential impacts on the people of Utah and residents of multiple downwind states.

Canadian businessman and television personality “Mr. Wonderful” (aka Kevin O’Leary) and his businesses have proposed the Stratos data center in Box Elder County, Utah, near Salt Lake City, that  will be one of the largest data centers in the world, requiring as much as 9 gigawatts (GW) of on-site power from burning methane gas. The Box Elder County Commission approved the project in May—under highly contentious circumstances and under a highly unusual arrangement within a project area created by Utah’s Military Installation Development Authority.

A new report prepared by EmPower Analytics Group for NRDC estimates that the air pollution, health harms, and economic damages from the proposed Stratos data center would be substantial: “[a]t the potential 9 GW full build-out, air pollution from Stratos’s methane-gas power generation is estimated to cause approximately 5–45 additional premature deaths and $75–$690 million in health-related economic damages every year.” The report also discusses additional health harms that extend beyond premature deaths and include new asthma cases, nonfatal heart attacks, hospital visits, and lost work and school days.

These ranges reflect scenarios that model the health harms and economic damage caused by air pollution from two possible methane-gas primary power generating technologies: modern combined-cycle combustion turbines and methane gas–fired reciprocating internal combustion engines (RICE). The primary harmful air pollution consists of tiny soot particles—particulate matter of 2.5 microns or less (PM2.5)—about 30 times smaller than the width of an average human hair.

The report further finds that over a 30-year period, at the potential 9 GW full build-out, the health and economic harms from this PM2.5 air pollution “could accumulate to approximately 856–1,350 premature deaths and $13.5–$20.7 billion under gas-fired RICE, compared with 145–253 premature deaths and $2.3–$3.8 billion under combined-cycle gas.”

Of the two methane-gas primary power generation technologies evaluated in the report, one uses a smaller number of large gas combustion turbines, while the RICE scenario reflected electricity generated by many individual engines operating in parallel. Indeed, the RICE scenario is patterned on a methane gas–powered data center with 69 RICE generators that the Utah Division of Air Quality permitted in Millard County in early 2026. RICE generators produce higher emissions than combined-cycle gas at the same generation capacity. Accordingly, the report finds that “[t]he technology used to power Stratos could make a five- to sixfold difference in its estimated health harms.”

Importantly, the modeled combined-cycle gas scenario does not estimate the emissions from the use of on-site diesel generators for backup power because those plans by Stratos remain unknown. (The report modeled diesel backup generators for the RICE scenario, scaled from the Millard County permit example.) Diesel generators are significantly dirtier than comparably sized methane gas generators, emitting 95 percent more smog-forming nitrogen oxides than an equivalent gas generator as well as 98 percent more non-methane hydrocarbons and 59 percent more carbon monoxide. Harmful PM2.5 emissions will be even higher from any data center employing diesel generators (the scale of that additional pollution would depend on whether they were truly used for “emergency conditions” versus being run more frequently; state permitting parameters for backup diesels is a critical issue for all data center developments).

The highest concentrations of harmful air pollution from the Stratos data center will occur near the facility in Box Elder County, neighboring areas of northern Utah (including Salt Lake City), and southern Idaho. But the health harms and economic damage from Stratos would extend well beyond these areas: The report finds that more than 80 percent of health harms and health-related economic damages occur outside the state, reaching downwind populations in Colorado, Wyoming, and as far away as Illinois. The report appendix identifies the full list of potentially impacted states.

The Stratos developers have maintained from the start that the massive data center will be powered by methane gas. “That’s never going to happen,” Utah Governor Spencer Cox told the Salt Lake Tribune in May. “The very first phase will be natural gas, but the other phases should not be. They should be nuclear, and they should be geothermal, and solar and other technology.” But the most recent filing by the Stratos developers with the state still show plans for the full build-out of the data center to be powered by methane gas. This filing shows that the developers now say that they plan a 7.5 GW data center using the dirtier and more harmful RICE generators that burn methane gas. None of these filings commit the developers to doing or not doing something different from what they are saying now, a reality that has characterized this data center project—and many other proposed data center projects nationally.

Data centers in Utah or anywhere else, of course, need not be powered by highly polluting, harmful fossil fuels like methane gas. Other data center developers have been doing better jobs showing how, in stark contrast to the Stratos proposal, it is possible to power these facilities with cleaner energy—such as a robust portfolio of wind, solar, geothermal, and batteries—and maxing out demand side resources.

NRDC commissioned this important EmPower Analytics report to raise awareness and concerns about the harmful health impacts and associated economic damages from dangerous air pollution caused by data centers like the proposed Stratos Project in Utah and by the proliferation of data centers across the United States. These harms arise especially from hyperscaler data centers powered by fossil fuels, like Stratos. While the dangers and costs of excessive water use by data centers are more well known, the deadly toll of air pollution—a silent killer—deserves greater attention and concern.

Harmful air pollution should be met with the best air pollution control technologies available. Data centers should be powered by new, additional, deliverable clean energy that does not release air pollution, along with batteries, storage, and other smart solutions. If someone nicknamed Mr. Wonderful wants to earn that name, he should live up to it, starting with the Stratos data center.

By John Walke, Director, Federal Clean Air & Senior Attorney, Environmental Health, NRDC

NRDC is the nation's most effective environmental action group, combining the grassroots power of 1.3 million members and online activists with the courtroom clout and expertise of more than 350 lawyers, scientists, and other professionals.

NRDC has 432 posts and counting. See all posts by NRDC

Your phone's apps could have more permissions than they need – here's how to check

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Staying on top of what information your apps can access is always a good idea.

It's great that we have access to hundreds of thousands of apps on our smartphones, but it's always worth being mindful of which apps you install. Though Android and iOS have surprisingly effective built-in security measures to detect and block malicious apps, some apps may still pose a risk if they have access to sensitive information or device features. An app requesting permission to access your camera, location, text messages or files doesn't necessarily mean it's unsafe, but it's worth asking why it needs them in the first place — especially if they have little to do with what the app is actually supposed to do.

Reviewing app permissions on your phone every once in a while is therefore a good way to keep your personal data secure, and thankfully, both Android and iOS let you do so fairly easily through their settings menus. If you don't want an app to access your location in the background, for instance, you can revoke that permission or change it so the app can only access your location when you're actively using it.

Your device's camera and location details are especially sensitive, which is why Android and iOS show on-screen indicators when an app is actively using your camera, microphone or accessing location. The iPhone displays a green dot when your camera is in use, an orange dot when an app is listening via the microphone and a blue location icon when an app pings your whereabouts. Android phones use similar status bar icons, and you can swipe down to access the Control Center or the quick settings panel to see which app is accessing these features.

Apple is known for being privacy-oriented, so it's no surprise that its operating systems have sophisticated privacy and security measures. Both iOS 27 and iPadOS 27 handle app permissions similarly. Any third-party app you install and use for the first time is required to request your permission before it can access information like your contacts, photos, location or microphone. When an app requests location access, you can tap Allow Once if you want to grant it temporarily, or select Allow While Using App if you trust the service.

You can also view and manage app permissions at any time by navigating to Settings > Privacy & Security. This section lists each permission type alongside the number of apps accessing it. For instance, tapping Microphone shows every app on your phone allowed to access it. You can revoke access for any app by turning the toggle off next to its name.

Certain apps like Google Maps or Uber might work optimally only if you've granted them the Precise Location permission and have set their location access to Always. You can dial these permissions back on a per-app basis, too. Similarly, when an app requests access to your camera roll, you can set the permission type to Limited Access and select only the photos and videos you want to share.

If you enable App Privacy Report, you can view which apps have accessed your phone's data and sensors. This section also lets you monitor the network activity for apps so you can see which domains they're contacting and when.

Android's privacy features have improved considerably over the years, and like iOS, you can view and manage permissions on a per-app basis. While the exact menu labels will vary depending on the manufacturer, you should be able to find detailed information on which apps have access to your camera, microphone, location, contacts and other data very easily.

The easiest way to revoke a specific app's permissions is to open its App info page. You can get there by long-tapping the app's icon and selecting the i icon. Alternatively, navigate to Settings > Apps > See all apps and select the app from the list. Tap on Permissions, select a data type and choose Allow or Don't allow. Some device permissions, like location, let you set the access type to All the time or Allow only while using the app.

Android also offers the Privacy dashboard, which lets you view different permission types and which apps have accessed them in the past 24 hours. Once again, the exact steps to get to this menu will vary. Try looking for this feature in the Security and privacy submenu in the Settings app, or type it into the search box.

Android apps must ask for device permissions when you first launch them, and you can either allow or deny these requests. Both the Play Store and the App Store also list what information an app may request access to, giving you another way to review permissions before installing it.

Democrats flip the Senate with stunning victories in deep-red terrain

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Democrats won control of the Senate on Tuesday, sending shock waves through Washington on a historic night that saw them flip seats in deep-red states such as Texas, South Carolina and Kansas.

Hours after The Associated Press called the Senate for Democrats, the party was projected to flip the House as well. As of 1:50 a.m. Wednesday, Senate Democrats had wrested seven seats from Republicans — a stunning result that gives the party new powers to confront President Donald Trump in his final two years in office.

Winning the Senate was considered an unlikely prospect for Democrats at the beginning of the year, given the number of red states they needed to compete in. But strong candidate recruitment and a favorable political environment put the chamber within Democrats’ grasp.

With their majority, Democrats are expected to serve as a significant check on Trump, who could now have a more difficult time advancing his nominees through the chamber.

“Against all odds, we Democrats have won the Senate, and we have five words for the American people: Relief is on the way,” Senate Minority Leader Charles E. Schumer said Thursday night in Washington.

“The American people reduced the red brick wall to a pile of bricks,” the New York Democrat added. “As a result, there is now a generational remake of the Senate map that is going to change America now and on into the future.”

The Democratic path to the majority ran through several deep-red states that have proved hostile to the party in recent years and that Trump carried by double digits in 2024.

In Texas, state Rep. James Talarico defeated scandal-plagued Republican state Attorney General Ken Paxton, becoming the first Democrat to win a Senate election in the Lone Star State since 1988. Paxton had defeated Republican incumbent John Cornyn in a primary runoff earlier this year.

In perhaps the biggest shocker of the night, South Carolina Democrat Annie Andrews defeated appointed Sen. Darline Graham, a shocking flip in a state that last elected a Democratic senator in 1998. Graham was appointed to the Senate in July after the death of her brother, Sen. Lindsey Graham, and ran for the full term with Trump’s strong endorsement.

And the longest Senate drought in the country ended Tuesday when Democrat Adam Hamilton, pastor of the nation’s largest United Methodist church, unseated Republican Sen. Roger Marshall in Kansas, a state that had not elected a Democrat to the Senate in 94 years.

Other flips came in onetime battleground states that had turned decidedly red in recent cycles. In Ohio, Democratic former Sen. Sherrod Brown defeated Sen. Jon Husted, who was appointed to the Senate to complete the unfinished term of Vice President JD Vance. And in Iowa, Democratic state Rep. Josh Turek defeated Republican Rep. Ashley Hinson to succeed retiring GOP Sen. Joni Ernst.

In blue-leaning Maine, a 30-year Senate career came to a close with Republican Sen. Susan Collins losing her bid for a sixth term to Democratic former state Senate President Troy Jackson.

The night began strongly for the party, with key holds in Georgia, where Sen. Jon Ossoff easily dispatched GOP Rep. Mike Collins, and New Hampshire, where Rep. Chris Pappas defeated a comeback bid by former Sen. John E. Sununu.

Former North Carolina Gov. Roy Cooper also gave Democrats their first flip of the night, defeating Republican Michael Whatley, the former Republican National Committee chairman.

Democrats also held the open seat in Minnesota, with Lt. Gov. Peggy Flanagan prevailing over former NFL sideline reporter Michele Tafoya.

A Democratic majority in the Senate could run into a president who has said he would become “Mr. Veto” against any legislation coming out of chambers controlled by the opposition party.

With Trump in that position, Democrats will likely push “for legislation that may set the agenda for the future in case a Democrat’s elected in 2028,” according to Christian Grose, a professor of political science and public policy at the University of Southern California.

But in a Tuesday night interview with CBS News, Trump also said, “My message is, congratulations to the Democrats, and I look forward to working with you for the good of our country.”

With control of the Senate, Democrats are also poised to block Trump’s nominees, escalating the opposition they mounted while in the minority during his first two years back in office.

“Very few of them are going to get through,” Schumer told Semafor in October. “It’s the quality of the candidate that matters. Thus far, Trump has a record of notoriously bad candidates.”

Those nominees would likely extend from the Cabinet to the judicial branch, potentially even hindering confirmation of a Supreme Court pick in case of a vacancy.

Schumer told reporters ahead of the October recess: “The bottom line is, one of the reasons we want to do everything we can to take back the Senate is not let Trump put such horrible justices — MAGA justices — on the Supreme Court and the lower courts.”

Other Democrats have agreed with that sentiment and pointed to 2016, when Mitch McConnell led the Senate and blocked the confirmation of President Barack Obama’s Supreme Court nominee Merrick Garland. That left the seat open for more than a year, with Trump eventually filling the spot with Neil Gorsuch.

As Sen. Ruben Gallego, D-Ariz., told reporters recently: “We’ve got to do exactly what McConnell did to us.”

Senate Democrats will also gain subpoena power, bolstering their ability to investigate Trump and his administration. Those investigations could include his handling of the conflict in Iran, his family’s business ties and wealth gains while in office, or his ties to convicted sex offender Jeffrey Epstein.

“We have a real duty to investigate all of this just absolutely blatant corruption that’s so dangerous to our national security,” Senate Judiciary member Richard Blumenthal of Connecticut said in September, shortly after reporting emerged about wedding after-party celebrations for Trump’s eldest son, which were financed by a Russian businessman with ties to the Kremlin.

While Democrats in Congress could in theory pursue a third impeachment against the president, the appetite to seal the deal in the Senate would likely fall short, given they would need 67 votes to convict.

John T. Bennett contributed to this report.

A Complete Guide To The Star Trek Set Phasers To Superdrop Secret Lair Drop

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

As we've come to expect with new Magic: The Gathering Universes Beyond sets, a whole Secret Lair superdrop is launching alongside the new release. For Star Trek, we're getting five bundles in total, covering everything from full-art basic lands to an entire Klingon language set.

Admittedly, this Universes Beyond superdrop has some really cool cards, so if you're not financially ruined by two sets releasing close together and the endless stream of Secret Lair releases, it offers great options for Trekkies who want to expand their Magic card collection.

The Star Trek Set Phasers To Superdrop Secret Lair bundles are dropping on Monday, November 9, at 9 am PT, 12 pm ET, 5 pm GMT, and 6 pm CET.

This will follow the same structure as typical Secret Lair superdrops, meaning all bundles will be available to add to a cart from 8 am PT, 11 am ET, 5 pm GMT, and 5 pm CET. At that point, you will be asked to log in to your Wizards account, then placed in a waiting room until the products go live.

At that time, the entire waiting room is randomized into a checkout queue. You will be given an approximate waiting time based on your position in the queue. When you are admitted onto the Secret Lair website, you have ten minutes to check out your cart. Some products may sell out, in which case they will be removed from your cart.

As for prices, each bundle costs $29.99 for the nonfoil variant and $39.99 for the foil version. Wizards of the Coast has yet to confirm any complete bundles, but we fully expect these to be available on the day. This includes bundles of just the foil or nonfoil cards, plus an everything bundle with both copies of each.

These bundles are rarely sold at a major discount; instead, they're conveniently packaged to make checkout easier.

Finally, this drop's promo card is Transporter Boondoggle, a reskinned version of Return of the Wildspeaker. You will earn one copy of this card for every $199 spent on the superdrop. Free shipping is available when you spend $99.

If you're getting ready to head to your LGS for Star Trek prerelease, then here's everything you need to know about the latest Universes Beyond set.

This gorgeous set of cards focuses on Star Trek II: Wrath of Khan, with the iconic villain played by Ricardo Montalbán featured on three of the six cards in the bundle. Overall, the bundle is a little unfocused, with two mono-blue cards, one mono-green, one mono-black, one white-black, and one mono-white.

However, if you're a big fan of the Wrath of Kahn film, the artwork on these cards is more than worth the cost of the bundle. Here are the six cards included in this bundle, two of which are renamed alternatives.

Next up is the Lower Deck bundle, which, unsurprisingly, centers on the Lower Deck animated show. This bundle features five cards, all of which are renamed, but it's just as varied as the Kahn bundle, with one colorless card, one green-blue, one mono-red, one black, one white, and one black-blue.

Here are the five cards from the Lower Decks bundle:

A surprising inclusion up next is the Tlhingan Mah bundle, which is exclusively Klingon language cards. Well, kinda. Each card in this deck is double-sided, with one side featuring the English translation and the other the Klingon alternative. These are all mono-red cards, making it the most focused bundle of the entire superdrop, bar the lands.

Here are the five cards from the Tlhingan Mah bundle:

Reversible cards are also the name of the game for the Beam Me Up bundle (not to be confused with the Beam Me Up sealed gift bundle). This set only contains four cards, but each is reversible with different artwork on each side of the card. It features two mono-white cards, one mono-blue, and one colorless.

Here are the four cards in the Beam Me Up bundle:

Finally, we have the New Worlds bundle, which contains ten cards in total, but it's two copies of each of the basic lands, which have been given a new Star Trek treatment. There's an argument that these could have been included in the main set, and we wouldn't expect many players to go out of their way to buy this bundle separately, but die-hard fans may appreciate the chance to bling out some of their favorite decks.

The Best Splurge-Worthy Gifts for the Hunter Who Has It All

We may earn revenue from the products available on this page and participate in affiliate programs. Learn more

Shopping for a hunter who seems to have everything? This is where you pull out the big guns (sometimes literally). We rounded up some of our favorite splurge-worthy gear, from premium optics and thermal scopes to backcountry packs, hunting waders, and even a few upgrades for camp. Our experts have put many of these to the test ourselves—and stand behind them—so you can feel good about going a little bigger with this gift.

Thermal optics let you see heat signatures in total darkness, which makes them some of the coolest (and most useful) gear a hunter can own. The Pulsar Thermion 2 Pro pairs a crisp thermal image with multiple viewing modes and built-in recording, so you can spot game and capture what you’re seeing in the field.

If you really want to spoil the hunter on your list, it’s hard to go wrong with these Swarovski binoculars. They deliver incredible glass clarity and built-in laser rangefinding, so you can spot game and get an accurate distance without switching between two pieces of gear. They’re definitely a splurge, but once you use them, it’s easy to see why they’re worth it.

If you know a serious duck hunter, these Sitka waders would make one heck of a gift. They’re built with tough yet breathable 4-layer Gore-Tex Pro fabric, a waterproof front zipper, and a redesigned boot that’s lighter and more comfortable for long days in the water. Tip: Snag them in the badass Dark Timber camo Sitka just dropped this month for a new look this season.

For the hunter with a growing gun collection (aren’t we all?!), we recommend our very own Field & Stream gun safe. It has enough room to hold 40 long guns, along with adjustable shelves, racks, and pouches to keep ammo and other shooting accessories secure and organized.

Montana Knife Company has built a following for its American-made blades, all crafted under the direction of master bladesmith Josh Smith. This deluxe three-piece set gives you a few great knives—all made with MagnaCut stainless steel and durable G10 handles—to have on hand for everything from processing venison to making dinner.

Electric bikes have become a popular way for hunters to cover more ground, haul gear, and reach spots that would otherwise mean a long hike. The Bakcou Mule is built for rugged terrain and heavy loads, making it a major upgrade for anyone who hunts big properties or hard-to-reach public land.

For hunters who spend long days covering steep, rocky terrain, a supportive pair of boots is a must. Kenetrek’s Mountain Extreme boots—which our experts dubbed the best elk hunting boots out there—have thick full-grain leather uppers, a waterproof breathable membrane, and a supportive midsole that helps keep your feet comfortable under a heavy load.

Stone Glacier has built a reputation for making some of the best backcountry hunting packs out there, and the Sky 5900 is easily its most popular model. It’s designed to haul heavy loads over long distances, with a lightweight frame, plenty of storage, and a versatile design that works well for multi-day hunts.

We recently had a chance to test out the Meat! vacuum sealer and let me tell you, this thing is legit. It’s a heavy-duty chamber sealer that pulls a strong, consistent vacuum and creates tight, reliable seals that help keep everything from backstrap to ground venison fresh in the freezer.

We put the Goal Zero Yeti 1500 through its paces in our testing and were impressed by how well it powered everything we plugged into it, making it a great pick for hunters who spend a lot of time at camp or off the grid. It has a 1,505Wh battery along with multiple AC and USB outlets for charging everything from phones to larger appliances. Bonus: There are several ways to recharge it, including solar!

Of all the dog training collars we’ve tested over the years, this Garmin combo is by far the best. It combines advanced GPS tracking with training features, two-way communication, and inReach satellite messaging to make communicating with and keeping track of your dog in the field a whole lot easier.

A good spotting scope lets you size up game from a distance without having to get closer and risk spooking it. We tested the Maven S1.2A and loved its sharp image quality and impressive low-light performance, which make it easier to pick out details when conditions aren’t ideal.

If you really want to go all out on a gift, the Orion Modular Hunting Blind is hard to top. We tested it and were impressed with the overall design, build quality, and comfort (plus, it’s easy to set up with zero machines or tools required!). It’s a significant investment, but for someone who spends a lot of time hunting from a blind, it is well worth it.

Ripple Treasury Links To SWIFT With XRP-Linked Hook

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Home » Ripple (XRP) » Ripple Treasury Links To SWIFT With XRP-Linked Hook

SWIFT’s ISO 20022 gold standard covers a lot of territory in terms of Ripple-linked banking systems.

A former SWIFT standards veteran with more than 27 years at the messaging network says ISO 20022 gives banks a practical way to handle digital assets without rebuilding their back offices.

At the same time, Ripple Treasury’s documented partnerships with SWIFT keep XRP in the conversation as traditional and blockchain rails continue to overlap.

Tom Alaerts, who spent the bulk of his career working on the standards that underpin global payments, has pushed back on the idea that blockchain will simply replace SWIFT.

In a recent conversation with SMQKE, the popular blockchain researcher, he argued that decades of trust, security, neutrality & governance still make the cooperative hard to displace.

My full conversation with Tom Alaerts, who spent over 27 years at SWIFT working on the standards the global payment system runs on.😶‍🌫️Every technical difficulty has been cut out. What is left in this video below is the complete conversation from start to finish, for anyone who… pic.twitter.com/WAFSDof848

What has changed, he said, is the standard itself. Updates around 2022 and 2023 made it easier to represent digital token identifiers, blockchain addresses & higher numerical precision inside the securities messages banks already use.

That means institutions can process tokenized assets through existing systems rather than standing up entirely new infrastructure.

SWIFT’s Tom Alaerts noted that ISO-compliant networks and fintechs face fewer hurdles when banks and central-bank systems evaluate them.

Because they already use terminology and data structures familiar to traditional finance, security reviews, resilience testing, and regulatory checks become less friction-heavy.

He fundamentally expects a hybrid environment in which traditional messaging and blockchain settlement coexist rather than one displacing the other.

🚨A former SWIFT expert with 27 YEARS of experience says ISO 20022 makes it EASIER for $XRP to integrate with GLOBAL BANKING. pic.twitter.com/VijDbUdsAS

For digital assets, SWIFT networks that already map cleanly to ISO 20022, that hybrid path is shorter.

Another SWIFT – Ripple related discussion has focused on Ripple Treasury, the platform that emerged after Ripple’s acquisition of GTreasury. Materials highlight strategic partnerships with leading banks, SWIFT, Refinitiv & other providers to support end-to-end corporate treasury workflows.

The platform sits inside SWIFT’s Certified Partner Program and offers connectivity options such as Alliance Lite2 along with tools for IBAN and ABA lookups. It also lets corporate users manage both traditional fiat balances and digital assets including XRP and RLUSD in one place.

The connection is at the treasury-platform level rather than a direct pipe from the XRP Ledger into SWIFT messaging, but it still places XRP inside a workflow that already touches the network banks rely on daily.

Taken together, the two threads point to the same practical outcome: banks do not need to abandon existing rails to experiment with digital assets.

SWIFT’s ISO 20022 gold standard supplies a shared language, while platforms such as Ripple Treasury supply the operational layer that can sit alongside those rails. SWIFT remains central because of the trust and governance already built around it.

Networks and assets that align with the standard, and that appear inside the tools corporate treasurers already use, are simply better positioned as the two systems continue to converge.

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

"See that mountain? You can overanalyze it."

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

When the Greybeards invite you to High Hrothgar in Skyrim, you have to climb a mountain to reach them. There's a messenger called Klimmek waiting in the village of Ivarstead at the bottom to warn you what an arduous journey this is going to be. To get to the top of the Throat of the World, he says, you have to work your way up The 7,000 Steps.

It's well-documented that there aren't actually 7,000 steps carved into the side of the mountain. Some of them are buried under the snow, making an exact count impossible, but the actual number is closer to 700. And even if you argue that The 7,000 Steps refers to the number of footsteps it takes to ascend the mountain, according to the dedicated researchers at the Ultimate Elder Scrolls Portal it only takes "approximately 2,400 footsteps" to get there.

As part of Bethesda's 40th anniversary, which included the reveal of The Elder Scrolls 6's subtitle, the studio reminisced about their previous work, which is how we know they really did try to get 7,000 steps into the side of the mountain.

While not responsible for the rough layout of Skyrim's terrain, I was responsible for refining the shapes of High Hrothgar. I would look at the mountain from various viewpoints and tried to keep the shape interesting. My work on the "Throat of the World" was not limited to sculpting the mountain, but also included cluttering and dressing the mountain with visual effects, plants, and objects. This of course, included the infamous 7,000 steps. Now, I love trying to be as faithful to lore or real-life as possible and I did my darndest to find a way to fit all 7,000 steps into the storied path. However, my best attempt to do so included extending far past Ivartstead, wrapping around the mountain and ending near Whiterun in the frozen tundra. It turns out, 7,000 is quite a lot! By the way, my work did NOT include enemy encounters, so I take no responsibility for the Frost Troll that was the woe of many players.

Of course, like most open world games, Skyrim isn't designed to scale—otherwise the capital city of Solitude would have a population of less than 100 people. But in the same way that for every named NPC we should assume there are a bunch of people off-camera, distances are compressed so that it doesn't take a week to get anywhere. For every step we watched the Dragonborn climb there are another nine implied steps we don't see, and even more frost trolls.

After Rebecca Yarros’ Comment About Fourth Wing Characters Dying, I'm Worried About Someone Specific

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Join The Club for quick access. Enter your email below and we'll send confirmation, and sign you up to our newsletter.

Keep exploring and earning more as a member.

Dexter: Resurrection's Season 2 Premiere Brought Back My Favorite Character, But The Show Is Repeating My Problem With Last Season

NCIS: Origins Cast Reacted To That ‘Terrifyingly Sad’ Revelation, And I’m Right There With Them

Unlock instant access to exclusive member features.

Get full access to premium articles, exclusive features and a growing list of member rewards.

Well, I wasn't worried about this person until now...

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

If there’s one thing I know about Rebecca Yarros and her Fourth Wing books, it’s that she’s not afraid to kill anyone. That was clear from the moment Violet and co. stepped onto the parapet, and it became painfully obvious after our dearly beloved Liam’s death in the first book. Now, as we wait for book four (and the book-to-screen adaptation that just found its Violet and Xaden), the author has revealed that she had plans to kill off more characters than she did in Onyx Storm, and they’ll have to go in the next installment. So, yeah, I’m worried, and I’m really anxious about one specific character.

Listen, I spiral over comments Yarros makes about what’s going to happen in her books even harder than I spiral over updates and fancasts for the Fourth Wing TV show. So, you can imagine the panic I was sent into when she said the following at Iron Flame Night (via domloves2read):

So, I know when I’m in the book, when I start the book, who is going to die. Now, when I wrote Onyx [Storm] I actually was going to kill off other people at the end. And the way I had to shift the plot, and the way I had to shift things so that it wouldn’t feel like other books and it would have its own separate ending, it left some people alive that should not be alive that I will have to take care of in this book.

Yarros went on to clarify that she didn’t tell the audience if said characters “were good or bad,” and she poked fun at them, saying they are prone to jumping to conclusions that often involve her “plotting Ridoc’s murder.” She also said she always knows who she’s going to kill in a battle before she writes it, and she plants seeds ahead of time so we feel connected to those characters.

So, with all that in mind, I’ve realized I’m actually super duper worried about one character who (at least for now) is alive.

I know, I know, I can’t believe I’m writing this either: I really think Rhiannon could die in book four, and maybe Yarros had plans to kill her in Onyx Storm. So, let’s walk through why.

I actually got to this conclusion through a weird process of elimination. I was mostly thinking about what characters are either dealing with loose ends or are such a vital part of the story that they can’t die. Think characters potentially involved in the brother mystery, like Bodhi and Garrick, or those involved with Violet’s memory loss, like Imogen. Along with that, characters like Aaric are so powerful that it feels like they have a greater purpose to serve.

Plus, with the death of Violet’s mom, I don’t think Brennan or Mira will go (yet). I also don't think Dain or Sloane will die, considering their seemingly budding relationship. Then, just from a vibes perspective, I don’t think Ridoc will die (yet). And Swayer has already been through too much with going through first year twice and losing his leg.

Through that process, considering whose death would hurt Violet the most and just thinking about what’s happened in the first three books, I landed on Rhiannon as the character who was maybe going to die in Onyx Storm.

Other than Xaden and her siblings, I think Rhi’s death would hit Violet the hardest since she’s her best friend.

Now, adding to all that, a brutal Fourth Wing parallel led me to this conclusion, too. Think about it, of all the people in Violet’s crew, Rhi is probably the best of the bunch. She’s a natural leader, she was born to be a rider, and she’s simply a very good person. You know who else was near-perfect and all those things? Liam. And he died toward the end of Fourth Wing.

Yarros specifically said she didn’t kill a character in Onyx Storm because she didn’t want it to feel like the end of other books. If Rhi had died, it could have felt a whole lot like what happened to Liam. It would hurt Violet in a very similar way, and it would be the death of another hero, a good one, in this story.

So, yes, going into book four, I’m very worried for Rhiannon. However, for now, I’m going to choose to be very happy that she’s still alive and will live to see another page (for now).

Riley Utley is the Weekend Editor at CinemaBlend. She has written for national publications as well as daily and alt-weekly newspapers in Spokane, Washington, Syracuse, New York and Charleston, South Carolina. She graduated with her master’s degree in arts journalism and communications from the Newhouse School at Syracuse University. Since joining the CB team she has covered numerous TV shows and movies -- including her personal favorite shows Ted Lasso and The Marvelous Mrs. Maisel. She also has followed and consistently written about everything from Taylor Swift to Fire Country, and she's enjoyed every second of it.

Aussie Adobe assailant Canva announces layoffs in re-org affecting 22% of staff

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

No mention of AI, but a nod to the pace of technological change

Australian design software maker Canva is restructuring, with 1,271 employees, or 22 percent of its workforce, facing layoffs, changes to their roles, or requests to relocate.

The company, which has become a challenger to Adobe, announced the news by sharing a message that CEO Melanie Perkins sent to staff on Wednesday.

That missive, titled “An important update on our team,” doesn’t mention AI but does state “New technology is transforming what people can create, how they create it, and what they expect from the products they use.”

Perkins observed that Canva’s rate of growth recently slowed for the first time in a decade and shared her view that continued success won’t happen without change.

“After exploring a wide range of alternatives, I've come to the difficult conclusion that small changes won’t be enough,” she wrote. “To set ourselves up for the next decade, we need to refound Canva, getting back to our roots of small, empowered teams with the freedom to make decisions, take ownership and focus on what matters most for our community.”

The CEO did not say how many jobs would go, but argued the changes are necessary because the company has become inefficient.

The changes will therefore see Canva combine its product, growth and platform teams into a single “Product Org,” which Perkins says means “changing the way we work to provide clearer ownership, greater autonomy and flexibility to tackle our most important goals.”

She characterized the changes as “simplifying layers, consolidating overlapping responsibilities, and ensuring teams have the right mix of skills and experience for the chapter ahead.”

The CEO three times described herself as “deeply sorry” for the changes.

Affected staff will receive a calendar invite for an optional individual meeting with a senior leader. Remaining staff get to attend two rounds of meetings next week – one company-wide affair and another with their reorganized teams.

Another of Perkins’ decisions will see Canva move more of its product teams to its headquarters in Sydney, Australia. The company will ask some staff to relocate and has promised “comprehensive support” to make that happen.

Departing staff can keep their company-issued MacBooks and will receive at least six months' pay, a year of health insurance, plus various forms of support to find a new gig including a one-year membership of LinkedIn Premium.

Perkins’ post does not cite financial pressures as a reason for the layoffs. Canva is privately held and does not face the same financial disclosure requirements as a listed business, but last year revealed it had 260 million monthly users and revenue of $3.5 billion. Execs have previously claimed the company is profitable on a free-cash-flow basis.

Canva and Atlassian are among Australia’s most successful and celebrated technology businesses. Both have now announced job cuts this year. ®

Donald Trump Says He'll Move to Israel and Be New PM If Voters Turn on Him

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

As millions of Americans head to the polls to cast their vote on Election Day, President Donald Trump is threatening to leave the United States altogether.

“If people ever get tired of me in the U.S., I’ll move to Israel and become prime minister,” the commander-in-chief, 80, claimed while calling into the Tuesday, November 3, episode of Fox & Friends and after touting his alleged popularity in the region.

“I’m at 99 [percent approval,” Trump claimed. “I have the highest approval rating of anybody in history.”

Trump’s apparent plans to leave the U.S. if he is no longer popular with the electorate comes amid the president’s record-low approval ratings. In a new CNN poll taken between October 13 and October 26, Trump’s disapproval rating in the United States was at 64 percent.

The president’s overall approval rating dropped to 34 percent. Just nine states still rate Trump positively, including Wyoming, North Dakota and Arkansas.

The president’s comments also come after he claimed the United States will no longer be sending military aid to Israel. (The U.S. sends approximately $4 billion in military aid to the country every year.)

“Israel’s our No. 1. We give them more than anybody else, and they no longer need it,” Trump said via phone during the on-air interview. “We’ve been doing it for years. I’ve been doing it. But Israel is a very thriving country. They don’t need it. If they did need it, I would say keep it going.”

Trump claimed to have recently made a deal with Israeli Prime Minister Benjamin Netanyahu for the United States to stop funding the country’s military, including the country’s missile-defense system the Iron Dome. The president alleged the pair “made a deal months ago” and that the money would get “cut off at the end of the year.”

“They’ve been a great partner, but we stopped Iran,” he said before changing topics drastically. “Iran was an unstoppable force. We knocked the hell out of them.”

Netanyahu, 77, has yet to publicly comment on the president’s claims.

Trump’s interview comes during a pivotal midterm election for the United States. Depending on the results in several key states, Democrats could take back control of both the Senate and the House of Representatives as Trump continues his second presidential term.

Hours before voting centers closed on the East Coast, Trump said he was watching Fox News and didn’t approve of one of the guests speaking.

“Watching the Pre-Election Results on Fox News, but I can’t stand Jessica Tarlov‘s voice — DUMB AS A ROCK, but it’s the voice that gets me,” he wrote via Truth Social on Tuesday. “Have to go somewhere else until that LOSER is off the air — I’ll be back later!”
