Tatyana Ali Jokes Her Sons Aren't 'Impressed' by Her 'DWTS' Performances

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Tatyana Ali is improving week after week on Dancing With the Stars, but to her sons, she’s still just mom.

When asked whether her sons, Edward Aszard “Aszi,” 10, and Alejandro, 7, are “impressed” by how much she’s progressed on season 35 of the ABC competition series, Ali, 47, exclusively told Us, “No, no, no, no. Listen, no, no, no, no. They’re not. No, forget that. They’re not. I’m their mother. They’re not impressed by me.”

The Fresh Prince of Bel-Air alum noted on Monday, November 2, that her kids are still watching and they are “learning” about the world of dance.

“My son asked me, he said, ‘Mommy, what kind of dance are you doing this week?’” Ali recalled. “I said, ‘Oh, I’m doing a contemporary.’ And I said, ‘You know [my partner] Jan, Mr. Ravnik? You know, he’s a champion at contemporary dance.’”

Ali said that her oldest son was knowledgeable about the genre of dance she was describing, responding, “Oh yeah, contemporary. Isn’t that the one that’s kind of like choose your own adventure?”

Jan Ravnik chimed in telling Us, “It’s true. You can do whatever you want to do.”

Although Ali teased that her boys will always see her as their mom, she was happy to report that they are tuning in and rooting for her to take home the mirrorball trophy.

“They tell me they’re proud of me when we do our nighttime prayers,” she shared. “They’re always like, ‘Let Mommy last another week. Like, let her do a good job. Let her remember her steps.’”

The actress revealed, “It’s just a really cool experience to be able to show my kids perseverance in real time.”

Ali, who shares her two children with husband Vaughn Rasberry, admitted that while they are aware of who Ravnik, 31, is, they don’t know him that well.

“He’s been on FaceTime a couple, like maybe one or two times,” she explained, adding that her kids are in school and don’t live in Los Angeles so they don’t frequent her practices.

Ali and Ravnik have found themselves at the bottom part of the leader board for multiple weeks, but during Tuesday’s Disney Night they received their first nine of the season. Their total score was a 33 out of 40.

Judge Carrie Ann Inaba even told Ali that her storytelling made her very emotional after the pair performed a contemporary dance to “Easy As Life” from Aida with guest artist Heather Headley performing live.

“I mean, that’s the whole point of performing, whether it’s acting, dancing, singing, whatever. You want to actually touch people,” Ali told Us. “So that actually did mean a lot.”

APOD: 2026 November 4 – The Not-So-Hidden Galaxy, IC 342

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Discover the cosmos! Each day a different image or photograph of our fascinating universe is featured, along with a brief explanation written by a professional astronomer.

Explanation: “I see” IC 342, the Hidden Galaxy! Today’s image peers through the plane of the Milky Way, through all its gas, dust and stars, to unveil this spiral galaxy. IC 342 (or Caldwell 5) is one of the brightest members of the IC 342/Maffei group of galaxies. Located about 10 million light years away, this group is quite close to the Local Group that includes our Milky Way. Inaccurate accounting of the crowded and dusty Milky Way foreground has led to misunderstandings in IC 342’s history, including the theory that the galaxy was ejected from the Local Group. IC 342 has its own dust intermittently blocking the glow of hydrogen gas, indicating the presence of bright, young stars. Magnetic field maps of IC 342 reveal that magnetic fields may contribute substantially to one of the biggest galactic puzzles: how do spiral arms form?Tomorrow's picture: open space

‘Bachelor’ Alum Melissa Rycroft Breaks Silence on 2nd DWI Arrest: ‘Ashamed’

The Bachelor alum Melissa Rycroft is speaking out for the first time following her second DWI arrest.

“First, please know how deeply ashamed and embarrassed I am, especially knowing how much this has affected my friends and family,” Rycroft, 43, wrote via Instagram on Tuesday, November 3. “I want to share something personal, and to be honest, I am very ashamed to share it, but I can’t hide it anymore.”

The former Dancing With the Stars winner claimed she has been “struggling with my mental health for quite some time.”

“It has completely overwhelmed me,” she continued. “I’ve tried to hide what I’ve been going through by putting on a smile, but behind the scenes, things have been much harder than anyone realized or that I have let on, even to my family.”

While Rycroft said it’s no excuse for her allegedly getting behind the wheel while intoxicated, she “finally” admitted that she needs “help.”

“I’ve fought this for a long time on my own, and it’s time to admit that I can’t do it anymore. I’m getting professional care,” she shared. “This is so hard to say and the same is still very much there, but I’m ready to feel like myself again, for me and for my family. They have stood by me through all of this, and they deserve honesty. They deserve me at my best. That’s what I’m working toward for all of us.”

Two months after pleading guilty to a 2025 DWI charge, Rycroft was arrested on November 1 for another alleged DWI.

According to records viewed by Us, Tarrant County Corrections Center in Texas held Rycroft on three charges issued earlier that day — driving while intoxicated, driving with an invalid license and a collision involving damage to a vehicle under $200.

Police claim Rycroft allegedly hit an ambulance and drove off before she was ultimately taken into custody.

“Please have some grace with me, if you have it in your heart, as I focus on trying to get healthy,” she said in her latest social media post. “And please respect my family’s privacy as they navigate this with me too. Thank you.”

Rycroft was a contestant on 2009’s season 13 of The Bachelor and got engaged to Jason Mesnick. Shortly after their on-air proposal, Mesnick, 50, left Rycroft, explaining he had true feelings for runner-up Molly Malaney. The duo later got married in 2010.

Rycroft, herself, married Tye Strickland and the pair share three children together.

Follow today’s midterm election results with the Apple News Live Activity

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

With votes now being counted in the US midterm elections, the Apple News app offers a convenient way to follow the results in real time. Here’s how it works.

Today is Election Day in the United States, with all 435 seats in the House of Representatives and 35 Senate seats up for election.

And with polls now closing and results starting to come in, Apple News is once again offering an easy way to keep track of the vote count, as it has done in recent elections.

As a refresher, Live Activities provide real-time updates from apps directly on your iPhone’s Lock Screen and, on supported models, in the Dynamic Island. This means you can keep track of information without having to constantly open an app.

U.S. users can open the Apple News app and then tap on the “Follow the 2026 midterms live” banner at the top of the screen.

Once enabled, the Live Activity will display election results as they become available, with updates throughout the vote count. This makes it possible to follow the election throughout the evening, even while using other apps on your iPhone.

For users who’d rather follow the results on their computers, Live Activities from your iPhone can also appear in your Mac’s menu bar through iPhone Mirroring, a feature introduced in macOS Tahoe 26.

You can also open Apple News for additional coverage and analysis of the 2026 midterm elections.

Before the Dawn in America’s Heartland

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

With hardly a cloud in sight, astronauts had a sweeping view of nighttime lights in the U.S. Midwest.

The predawn hours brought clear skies over much of the eastern United States on October 18, 2026. An astronaut aboard the International Space Station seized the opportunity to capture this wide-angle photo at 4:54 a.m. Central Daylight Time (09:54 Universal Time), with illuminated cities large and small arrayed across the darkened landscape.

The U.S. Midwest’s largest metropolitan areas, such as Chicago, Illinois, and Detroit, Michigan, stand out as some of the brightest features. Mid-sized cities—including Indianapolis, Indiana; Cincinnati, Ohio; and Louisville, Kentucky—and their interconnections along state and interstate highways also shine brightly.

Above the web of nighttime lights and Earth’s lower atmosphere, a diffuse layer of green and orange is visible along the horizon to the left. This band of light is airglow, produced when sunlight strikes atoms and molecules in the upper atmosphere and causes them to eject photons. To the right, light from the Sun arcs across the horizon; sunrise was about an hour away on the East Coast and about two hours away in Chicago.

Astronaut photos and other remote sensing observations of nighttime lights are scientifically valuable for tracking changes in the built environment. For example, Chicago's lightscape appears a homogeneous pale yellow in the wide view above. But in detailed photos, like the one below, a range of colors stemming from different types of lighting is discernible. Using NASA’s extensive Gateway to Astronaut Photography of Earth archive, researchers have developed a method for distinguishing lighting types within the pixels of nighttime photos. Systematically quantifying urban lights’ brightness, color, and extent can provide insight into how cities grow and evolve.

Satellite sensors also observe changes to lighting. In particular, NASA’s Black Marble nighttime lights product suite uses satellite observations to produce science-quality records of nighttime lights across the globe at daily, monthly, and yearly time scales. In the eastern U.S., scientists have used these data to track everything from power outages and restoration following major storms to lighting changes associated with new infrastructure to transitions to LED lighting from older forms.

Astronaut photograph ISS075-E-226709 was acquired on October 18, 2026, with a Nikon Z9 digital camera using a focal length of 14 millimeters, and photograph ISS070-E-105097 was acquired on March 1, 2024, with a Nikon D5 digital camera using a focal length of 400 millimeters. They are provided by the ISS Crew Earth Observations Facility and the Earth Science and Remote Sensing Unit at NASA Johnson Space Center. The images were taken by members of the Expedition 75 and Expedition 70 crews, respectively. The images have been cropped and enhanced to improve contrast, and lens artifacts have been removed. The International Space Station Program supports the laboratory as part of the ISS National Lab to help astronauts take pictures of Earth that will be of the greatest value to scientists and the public, and to make those images freely available on the internet. Additional images taken by astronauts and cosmonauts can be viewed at the NASA/JSC Gateway to Astronaut Photography of Earth. Story by Lindsey Doermann.

WestJet Vs. Porter Airlines: Which Carrier Is More Premium In 2026?

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Canadian air travel has long been defined by a strict dual-class hierarchy, with flag carriers drawing a hard line between elite business class amenities and standard main cabin seating. However, as Porter Airlines aggressively expands its fleet of Embraer E195-E2 jets from coast to coast, the definition of a premium domestic flight has undergone a dramatic transformation. WestJet counters by refining its Boeing 787-9business class and rolling out high-speed Starlink WiFi across its narrowbody fleet, so now, Canadian travelers face two fundamentally different visions of luxury.

Deciding which airline holds the true premium crown depends entirely on whether a passenger values front-of-cabin hard product hardware or an upgraded baseline economy experience. As both carriers vie for market share across transcontinental and transborder routes, comparing their cabin products, seat pitches, and inflight services is more important than ever before booking.

WestJet offers the superior high-end premium product, while Porter Airlines delivers a far more upscale everyday experience for the average passenger. WestJet’s top-tier product is its business class cabin on the 787-9, which features fully lie-flat seats, direct aisle access, premium dining, and dedicated lounge access on long-haul routes. Conversely, Porter does not fly widebody aircraft or offer lie-flat seats, instead focusing on what it markets as an elevated economy experience across its entire fleet.

The fundamental difference lies in how each airline treats the main cabin. As per Seat Maps, Porter’s E195-E2 jets feature a two-by-two seating configuration with no middle seats, complimentary beer and wine served in real glassware, premium Canadian snacks, and fast, free WiFi for every passenger. WestJet’s narrowbody Boeing 737 fleet sticks to a traditional three-by-three layout in economy, where middle seats remain standard and buy-on-board food and drink policies apply.

Ultimately, WestJet wins for long-haul luxury and dedicated premium seating upfront, whereas Porter wins for overall comfort and inflight amenities in the main cabin. For anyone seeking true intercontinental luxury, complete with fully lie-flat suites, direct aisle access, multi-course dining, and dedicated airport lounge access, WestJet’s 787-9 fleet provides a full-service hard product that Porter cannot match. However, for standard domestic economy travel, Porter completely reshapes expectations by removing the dreaded middle seat across its entire E195-E2 fleet.

On narrowbody routes, WestJet’s premium product is WestJet Premium, situated in the forward rows of its 737s. This dedicated cabin features much wider recliner seats in a two-by-two layout, 37 inches to 38 inches (94 centimeters to 97 centimeters) of seat pitch, hot meal choices, and complimentary alcoholic drinks served on multi-hour flights.

Porter takes a different hardware approach with its top fare, PorterReserve. Rather than installing wider business-class seats, PorterReserve utilizes the same two-by-two seat frame found throughout the E195-E2 cabin, but expands seat pitch substantially to 36 inches (91 centimeters) across the first four rows. Instead of a wider chair, PorterReserve is a fully bundled, all-inclusive fare tier that includes fresh meals, pre-mixed cocktails, dedicated airport check-in, priority security screening, early boarding, two free checked bags, and fee-free flight changes.

On shorter domestic hops, PorterReserve frequently delivers comparable legroom and superior soft-product perks at a fraction of the cost of traditional business class. However, on transcontinental flights exceeding four hours, flyers seeking a true widebody recliner or lie-flat bed will find WestJet’s hardware significantly more comfortable.

They are from four Canadian airports. Discover them all here!

Aviation analysts and industry reviewers frequently frame PorterReserve not as a traditional business class, but as an enhanced, all-inclusive economy product. A September 2026 review by Frugal Flyer noted that even though PorterReserve falls short of international long-haul business class standards, citing cold meals served in cardboard packaging and standard seat padding, its price-to-value ratio directly challenges Air Canada’s domestic business class and WestJet Premium. The reviewer concluded that Porter is best understood as an upgraded, hassle-free way to fly economy without surprise add-on fees.

Porter’s strategy of democratizing cabin comfort has earned substantial industry accolades. Passengers voted Porter an APEX Four Star Major Airline, while the carrier also earned USA Today’s Readers’ Choice award for Best Economy Class and was named Skytrax’s Best Regional Airline in North America.

Meanwhile, WestJet continues to defend its premium positioning by expanding its long-haul Dreamliner network across Europe and Asia, operating non-stop routes as long as Calgary International Airport (YYC) to Rome Fiumicino Airport (FCO). WestJet executives emphasize that their two-tier strategy, combining international widebody luxury with a high-density regional fleet, provides the full-service flexibility that global business travelers demand, as reported by AvioRadar.

When compared with Canada's flag carrier, Air Canada, both WestJet and Porter fill distinct operational niches in the domestic and transborder markets. Air Canada’s Signature Class on Boeing 787, 777, and Airbus A330 aircraft remains the traditional benchmark for long-haul business class in North America, featuring private executive pods and extensive Maple Leaf Lounge networks. However, on domestic and transborder narrowbody routes, Air Canada’s standard economy cabin features the same three-by-three seating grid and buy-on-board food service found across WestJet’s 737 fleet.

Against low-cost alternatives like Flair Airlines, Porter and WestJet sit in a completely different tier of reliability and service. Ultra-low-cost carriers strip away all passenger amenities to offer bare-bones transport, whereas Porter elevates the baseline flight experience without charging flag-carrier premium prices. Meanwhile, WestJet approaches its own soft product differentiation by offering a spectrum of products ranging from ultra-basic economy up to widebody transcontinental business suites.

This positioning explains why customer choice varies so much by market segment. For price-sensitive travelers wanting an uncompromised economy flight, Porter beats both Air Canada and WestJet on value. However, for corporate travelers who rely on alliance connections, global lounge access, or international lie-flat seats, traditional network carriers like Air Canada and WestJet are still considered the necessary choice.

Montreal's new $450 million airport, YHU, aims to avoid Mirabel's pitfalls by focusing on domestic convenience rather than international prestige.

It is clear that Porter’s product highlights are compelling, but there are very real and impactful trade-offs that exist, trade-offs that travelers really need to evaluate carefully before booking. Network scale and schedule redundancy are the major factors to keep a close eye on as Porter’s jet fleet and route map, though expanding rapidly across North America, is a fraction of the size of Canada's traditional market leaders. If an aircraft suffers a mechanical delay or severe weather cancellation, WestJet’s broader fleet and dense flight schedules offer far more daily rebooking options to help stranded travelers recover. Passengers on thin Porter routes may face significantly longer wait times for the next available seat during irregular operations.

Rapid network expansion also introduces operational strain that directly impacts schedule reliability. Industry tracking data from Cirium records Porter’s on-time arrival rate trailing competitors at 71.9%, compared to WestJet’s 73.6% on-time performance. In real-world terms, this gap means a higher likelihood of flight delays for passengers working around tight connection windows or strict business itineraries. Combined with Porter’s leaner fleet infrastructure, a single delayed turnaround can trigger compounding schedule slippage throughout the day, whereas WestJet's operational scale lets it manage aircraft swaps and delay buffers more effectively across its main hub channels.

None (Embraer E195-E2 / Dash 8-Q400 only)

2-2 across entire jet fleet (zero middle seats)

Passengers that are accustomed to traditional business-class perks should note that PorterReserve does not grant access to third-party airport lounges. Those seeking quiet terminal spaces or complimentary meals before departure must rely on independent credit card lounge passes or opt for WestJet Premium and Business fares. Additionally, dual-airport cities present routing traps, as Porter operates out of both Toronto Pearson International Airport (YYZ) and Billy Bishop Toronto City Centre Airport (YTZ), meaning travelers need to double-check departure locations to avoid costly airport mix-ups.

The rivalry between WestJet and Porter Airlines in 2026 shows a clear division of passenger priorities across North American skies. If your primary goal is securing a true lie-flat suite on an international flight, or sitting in a wide, dedicated recliner seat with lounge access on a long transcontinental route, WestJet offers the superior hardware product.

However, for short-haul regional routes and transcontinental travel in standard economy, Porter delivers an unmatched base experience. With zero middle seats, free fast Wi-Fi, and an amazing drinks service included for every single passenger, Porter has successfully raised the bar for everyday domestic air travel.

Found an error? Send it info@simpleflying.com so it can be corrected.

When choosing between the two Canadian carriers, the best way to make the right choice is to take a closer look at specific aircraft types and total route duration. For quick two-to-three-hour hops, Porter’s standard cabin or PorterReserve fare offers extraordinary overall value. For long-haul intercontinental journeys where seat width and flat-bed rest are paramount, WestJet’s premium cabins will be the definitive choice.

What Is the UNESCO Tentative List, and How Do You Get on It?

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

With a total of 27 UNESCO World Heritage Sites, the United States is peppered full of sites of wonder. There's the Statue of Liberty, the patinated icon that has served as the first face millions of immigrants saw once they reached the country's shores and the San Antonio Missions in Texas which were first constructed the 18th century. Beyond manmade wonders, UNESCO has also recognized natural sites such as the Florida Everglades and the Grand Canyon. There's no shortage of modern marvels and breathtaking landscapes to behold in the US, which leads us to ask, how does a destination gain recognition as a UNESCO World Heritage Site? The process begins with the UNESCO Tentative List, which puts the best of the best up for consideration.

The Tentative List, as the name suggests, comprises natural and cultural properties that a country considers to have the potential to be recognized as World Heritage. It is not a confirmation that a site has been selected for inscription, only the first step towards a formal nomination, which is drawn up subsequently in the process. To qualify for World Heritage status, a property must demonstrate Outstanding Universal Value (OUV), meaning its cultural or natural significance ought to be considered exceptional from an international perspective. A site’s inclusion on the Tentative List places it in the position to be examined further for its potential OUV. This process enables a stronger foundation of vetting for the final nominations that a country draws up.

Even if a site included in a Tentative List is not nominated during one evaluatory round, there’s always a chance in the future that it may be added back to the list and considered for a nomination. The list is updated at any time per the country’s discretion, but each included site must undergo a separate nomination and evaluation process before qualifying for the World Heritage List. Once a site is inscribed as UNESCO World Heritage, it is removed from the Tentative List.

At the heart of UNESCO’s World Heritage process is the concept of Outstanding Universal Value (OUV). In simple terms, this means a place must be significant beyond its own country, and its cultural or natural importance must be exceptional enough to matter to people around the world, including future generations. A site must demonstrate this value against at least one of UNESCO’s 10 selection criteria, whether for its contribution to human history and culture, its geological features, its ecological processes or its biodiversity.

The cultural criteria include sites that represent exceptional examples of human creativity, demonstrate an important exchange of human values, bear testimony to cultural traditions or civilizations, or illustrate significant stages in human history. They also cover traditional settlements and landscapes, as well as properties associated with events, living traditions, beliefs, and works of outstanding universal significance.

The natural criteria consider exceptional natural beauty, significant stages in the Earth’s geological history, ongoing ecological and biological processes, and habitats important for conserving biodiversity.

Meeting one or more criteria is not enough on its own. A proposed property must also satisfy the relevant conditions of authenticity and integrity, alongside requirements for protection and management. Its inclusion must make a case for why its significance is exceptional in a global context, including through comparisons with similar properties.

It’s important to note that UNESCO’s 10 criteria are regularly revised to reflect the evolving understanding of World Heritage.

Countries identify properties they consider to have potential OUV and may eventually nominate for World Heritage status. UNESCO encourages them to involve a broad range of stakeholders in preparing their Tentative Lists, including site managers, local communities, government bodies, and non-governmental organizations. The process is led by each country, so the extent and nature of consultation can vary.

Currently, the US has 17 sites on their Tentative List, with nominations dating from 2008 and 2017. The US has submitted both cultural and natural sites for consideration with locales raning from the country's Pacific territories to Alabama. Below are the current contenders on the Tentative List, according to the National Park Service.

The country must still decide whether to proceed with a formal nomination and prepare the documentation required to demonstrate the property’s OUV. This includes establishing how the site meets the relevant selection criteria, documenting its authenticity and integrity, outlining the measures in place to protect it, and presenting a management framework. The nomination must also explain how the property compares with other sites of similar significance.

The country, not UNESCO, decides which properties to nominate for World Heritage status. Once a site on the Tentative List is formally nominated, independent advisory bodies assess its significance and conservation measures. The UNESCO World Heritage Committee then makes the final decision on whether to inscribe it on the World Heritage List.

Jurassic Park's Lost Animated Series Pitch Has Finally Surfaced In Video Form

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

It's been over 30 years since "Jurassic Park" changed movies forever, with Steven Spielberg's 1993 adaptation of Michael Crichton's sci-fi thriller novel delivering not only a perfect film, but a production that brought a systemic change to how Hollywood made movies. It also marked the last time a dinosaur franchise would become a bonafide hit, as Hollywood continues to try and fail to make audiences care about dinosaurs unless the title includes the word "Jurassic." (David Robert Mitchell's "The End of Oak Street" is the latest example of audiences largely avoiding non-"Jurassic" dino movies.)

Given how incredibly successful the "Jurassic Park" franchise has been, it's no wonder it's expanded to include spin-offs, including video games, cartoons, and more. But what is surprising is just how slow this franchise has been to grow. After all, the first film came out in 1993, at a time when practically every big movie somehow got turned into a cartoon, regardless of whether the original was for kids or not.

"Jurassic Park" did almost get an animated series back then, but it never made it to production. Still, we've heard about this show on and off over the years, and now, we finally have the very first bit of footage, reportedly titled "Escape From Jurassic Park." The trailer surfaced on Twitter/X, and it quickly spread. Watch for yourself right below.

After all of these years, it has finally been found!

Fans of Jurassic Park, I humbly present to you, the animated Pitch Trailers for the canceled Jurassic Park Animated Show!

More info coming soon! pic.twitter.com/UGT5lOvvd5

— Jurassic Library (@JurassicLibrary) October 31, 2026

The trailer for the "Jurassic Park" animated film, thought lost forever, doesn't show much, but it does perfectly capture the look and tone of the Spielberg classic and mixes it with that recognizable '90s Amblin animated look we saw in "Animaniacs" and other shows of the time.

There's not a whole lot of information about the lost "Jurassic Park" animated series. We do have old pitch decks and story treatments that give an idea of what the series could have been about, as well as old concept art from cartoon veterans Will Meugniot and artist William Stout, who were meant to spearhead the show.

Perhaps most importantly, the "Jurassic Park" TV series was meant to be a direct sequel to the first film, with the first season reportedly focusing on BioSyn creating its own rival dinosaur theme park, leading to — you guessed it — another dino outbreak.

According to William Stout, this "was not going to be a kiddy show (although kids of all ages, including myself, could enjoy it)," he said in his blog back in 2014. "They wanted the show to be a mature prime time series with top writers and state-of-the-art television animation augmented with quite a bit of CG animation."

That much is clear from the leaked trailer for the "Jurassic Park" animated series. Though it still features kids, a big sense of adventure and wonder, and a visual style reminiscent of Don Bluth, the trailer also makes it clear this is a show about man-eating dinosaurs, and seems to hint at an appropriate amount of violence.

We also know from reports about the series bible and its proposed episodes that the "Jurassic Park" cartoon would have been serialized, with each episode building up to the next and the first season ending with the rival park opening. That's a quite approach surprising for 1993, when serialization was virtually unheard of in animation.

Though the series never made it to production, we eventually did get a "Jurassic Park" cartoon (seen in the photo above), and it remains the best sequel to that original movie.

Your desktop's front USB ports may be slowing down every device you plug into them

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Having USB ports on the front of your desktop is convenient, but they might cost you power and speed.

Most desktop computers have USB ports on the front, which is convenient. Instead of reaching around to the back of the case and fumbling for the right port every time you need to connect a device, you can plug in and unplug your accessories in no time.

This convenience comes with a catch, though. Depending on what you connect to these ports, you may notice that the external device runs slower than it should: your files will take longer to copy and your phone won't juice up as quickly. This isn't a problem with your computer, but rather stems from how front-facing USB ports are engineered in your machine. It's also not a universal rule, as there are modern PCs with high-end ports on the front.

In a lot of cases, you probably won't realize the difference. But if you do notice it, there's a reason why the front ports of your computer are a little bit slower.

To understand this issue, we need to take a closer look at USB specifications and how motherboards are built. The USB ports on the back of your computer are usually soldered directly to the motherboard, so power and data travel a shorter path over them. Front ports, on the other hand, are connected via extension cables to reach the I/O controllers. Each additional layer in this connection results in signal loss, even if minimal.

Also, most high-end motherboards have more headers for USB ports, which means you can have multiple ports running at higher speeds. But front-panel ports are a different story; in many cases, they share a single connector, which means speed and power are split among all the ports on the front of your PC case. That's all in addition to manufacturers often using slower USB standards for at least some of the front ports.

Take the ASUS ROG Maximus Z790 Hero motherboard as an example — its rear has two 40 Gbps Thunderbolt 4 ports, as well as six 10 Gbps USB 3.2 Gen 2 ports and four more 5 Gbps USB 3.2 Gen 1 ports. While the front panel does have one USB 3.2 Gen 2x2 (theoretically 20 Gbps) port, it also has four USB 3.2 Gen 1 (5 Gbps) ports powered by two headers. That means the available speed is split among the connected devices as a shared pool. On some boards, if you connect two external SSDs to the front at the same time, they will share the same 5 Gbps data transfer rate.

Even Macs have these limitations. The current baseline Mac mini with the M6 chip has two USB-C ports on the front that offer speeds of up to 10 Gbps. But on the back, it has Thunderbolt 4 ports that reach speeds of up to 40 Gbps. The same applies to the latest Mac Studio, with the base model featuring two 10 Gbps USB-C ports on the front and Thunderbolt ports only on the back. The front USB ports are useful, but slower.

Whether this difference in power and speed will actually affect your peripherals depends on what you're using and what you need. For common devices like a mouse, keyboard or headphones, the ports on the front of your computer handle them just fine. These devices use very little power and don't require high speeds to run. Even a regular USB flash drive is fine to use with them if you don't plan on transferring huge amounts of data or constantly reading and writing.

Choose the rear ports when you're connecting bandwidth-intensive or latency-sensitive peripherals, such as external SSDs, capture cards or VR headsets. Plugging these into high-speed ports will definitely make a difference, and some of them may not even work on slower ports. It's definitely worth checking the specs of your motherboard or computer to see the exact speeds of each USB port so you'll know which one to plug each accessory into. Symbols or colors can give you a hint, but they aren't always consistent.

Colorado cleared to enforce assault rifle restrictions

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

The gun restrictions took effect in Colorado in August, limiting ownership of semiautomatic rifles to those who completed a safety course.

DENVER (CN) â A federal judge on Wednesday denied gunownersâ request to block enforcement of a Colorado law banning rapid-fire devices and restricting ownership of semiautomatic firearms to individuals who passed a safety course.

âThe court concludes that plaintiffs have not demonstrated that they are substantially likely to succeed on the merits of their Second Amendment challenge,â Senior U.S. District Judge William Martinez wrote in a 35-page opinion.

âAccordingly, plaintiffs are unlikely to suffer irreparable injury, which rises and falls here with its likelihood of success on the merits. Finally, the balance of the equities and public interest â Â construed together â tips in favor of defendants,â the Barack Obama appointee added.

Lawmakers passed SB25-3, the âAct Concerning the Prohibition of Certain Semiautomatic Firearms and Rapid-Fire Devices,â in 2025. The law took effect in August with first-time violators being penalized with a class 2 misdemeanor and subsequent infractions rising to a class 6 felony.

In order to purchase a semiautomatic weapon in Colorado, the law now requires individuals to complete a hunter education class with a four-hour safety course, or an extended 12-hour class spanning two days. The class must be taught by a sheriff-verified firearms instructor, with the final exam results recorded in a statewide database.

The sheriff is generally required to issue an eligibility card to anyone who completes the required coursework, but may deny a card if they have âreasonable belief that the documented previous behavior by the applicant makes it likely the applicant will present a danger to themselves or others.â

Aiming to block the law, six individuals and the Colorado State Shooting Association sued Governor Jared Polis and Attorney General Philip Weiser, both Democrats. Weiser won term-limited Polisâ position in Tuesdayâs election.

In addition to citing concerns over the state law handing off implementation to county sheriffs, the gunowners cited the high monetary and temporal cost of complying with the law as burdens that warranted a preliminary injunction.

To make their case, the gunowners pointed Martinez to the 2022 U.S. Supreme Court decision in New York State Rifle & Pistol Association v. Bruen. Under the high court’s two-step test, plaintiffs must first establish their conduct is protected by the Second Amendment, then the government is required to demonstrate its restriction is âconsistent with the principles that underpin our nation’s historical tradition of firearm regulation.â

Citing the Third Circuit, the gunowners argued semiautomatic rifles are in commonly use and therefore protected by the Second Amendment. The state, however, pointed to decisions in the Fourth and Seventh Circuits concluding âthe AR-15 is a combat rifle that is both ill-suited and disproportionate to self-defense. It thereby lies outside the scope of the Second Amendment.â

The U.S. Supreme Court is expected to settle the circuit split this term. In the meantime, however, Martinez concluded the gunowners failed to show âlaw-abiding citizens commonly use specified semiautomatic firearms today for self-defense.â

âNot only are these semiautomatic firearms particularly devastating to living tissue, not to mention commonly used in mass shootings; but there is also limited evidence to support them being necessary weapons for the self-defense of civilians,â Martinez wrote.

Because the gunowners failed to overcome Bruenâs first step, Martinez did not evaluate whether the state law adequately compared to historical regulations.

The firearm advocates are represented by the Mountain States Legal Foundation in Lakewood, Colorado and First & Fourteenth in D.C. They did not immediately return an inquiry for comment.

The Colorado Attorney Generalâs Office did not immediately respond to an inquiry for comment.

âWe have our lost revenue, our lost business opportunities, our lost goodwill and our lost time in the market,â Institute for Justice attorney Marco Vasquez Jr., representing the companies, said.

Wildtype produces cultivated salmon while UPSIDE produces cultivated chicken. Both products are made by growing animal stem cells into edible tissue and have received federal clearance to sell nationwide.

The Texas law prohibits the sale of âcell-cultured proteinâ in the state until Sept. 1, 2027. The companies contend the ban violates the dormant commerce clause and is preempted by the federal Poultry Products Inspection Act.

Vasquez pointed to statements by the billâs sponsor that âthe goal of this bill is to protect our agriculture industry.â

He also noted the lawâs statement of intent describing its purpose as supporting traditional agriculture. âSB 261 is no different from the law in [another] case that this court upheld that banned the sale of horse meat in Texas,â he said. âIt treats all in-state and out-of-state companies the same by way of a blanket ban as the court said there. âThere is no basis on this record to conclude that these two products or these two different types of entities are similarly situated,â he said.

He also characterized the companiesâ Texas sales as minimal â one restaurant relationship for Wildtype and a single sale to one resident for UPSIDE more than two years ago â and argued that any harm is speculative and outweighed by the stateâs interest in protecting residents from imperfectly understood risks.

The panel questioned the weight of legislative statements about protecting agriculture versus statements about potential long-term health risks of a novel product.

Duncan described the two-year sunset as a reasonable pause to allow further study.

Vasquez responded that Texas had produced no evidence of health risks and that federal regulators had already reviewed the companiesâ safety data.

The panel â which included U.S. Circuit Judges Don R. Willett, Andrew S. Oldham and Cory T. Wilson, all three appointed by Donald Trump â took the case under advisement. The ban remains in effect while the appeal is pending.

Booking Holdings took a $900 million revenue reduction and accrued a $100 million loss in the third quarter tied to a potential Federal Trade Commission complaint against its Priceline brand, which the company said is in discussions with the FTC to resolve. In a quarterly filing Tuesday, Booking said the FTC may seek restitution and penalties exceeding $1 billion, plus litigation and similar claims in other jurisdictions, and that litigation remains possible. The company disclosed in the second quarter that the FTC intended to file a complaint against Priceline and a B2B affiliate partner over disclosures, fees, customer support, and billing practices; Reuters reported last month, citing anonymous sources, that the partner is Guest Reservations. The FTC matter runs alongside a European Commission investigation that has called Booking.com's practices for combating fake listings and phishing "substandard." Operationally, net income fell 1% to $2.7 billion on revenue of $8.9 billion, also down 1%, while room nights rose 7%, accelerating from 5% in the second quarter. CFO Ewout Steenbergen described the fourth-quarter travel environment as "mixed," with moderation in international demand and domestic travel holding up as travelers take shorter trips closer to home. Connected trip transactions — bookings across two or more verticals — grew almost 20% year over year and made up a low double-digit share of Booking.com transactions.

Valve admits it's 'running behind' on approving Steam games because it has to keep re-reviewing them, which surely has nothing to do with AI

As wishlisted games miss their release dates, Valve creates a new checklist to help devs get it right the first time.

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

A new kind of Steam problem has emerged: Games keep missing their release dates because Valve can't keep up with approving them.

Several frustrated indie devs have publicly posted about the limbo status of their finished games in the past week—including the developers of Food Devils, Wheel Make It, and Cubic Earth—saying that their applications have gone unanswered after up to 20 days, despite Valve's listed review window of three to five business days.

In the case of Cubic Earth, developer structmira hasn't been able to contact Valve and has no choice but to wait. "Our game's trailer amassed more than 60,000 views on YouTube, and we had more than 6,000 wishlists," the developer told PC Gamer over email. "Now, our Steam Community page is rapidly filling with player complaints, and we've lost all momentum."

Valve commented on the backlog today in the form of a Steamworks update aimed at helping developers pass the preview process. In it, the company admitted that it's "running a little behind" with wait times that are up to double the usual.

"We aim to test every game build in a timely manner, typically within 3-5 days. However, we're running a little behind right now and taking up to 12 business days," Valve's FAQ reads. "Much of that delay is due to having to re-review games, which isn't great for you or for us."

The update itself is, primarily, a new series of checklists that places the onus on developers to submit better applications that "pass review quickly the first time." The most significant new requirement is to have "run your game through Steam for at least five minutes" to make sure it functions correctly, which you'd think wouldn't need to be said. The tone of Valve's request suggests that it's also surprised by the state of some of the games it's being asked to approve..

"Believe it or not, sometimes we review builds that have not ever been played through Steam," the update reads. "This means that even if a game runs smoothly when a dev launches their executable directly, they might have missed basic steps that would impact a user installing and playing via Steam."

From the creators of Edge: A free weekly videogame industry newsletter, featuring in-depth news, expert analysis and exclusive interviews

Other checks include confirming which of those "common redistributables" a game needs before submission, confirming cloud saves and achievements are in order, and adding a minimum of five screenshots to the store page that depict gameplay (another obvious, but apparently necessary standard).

Though Valve did not comment on why a logjam in its processes has developed only recently, despite the number of new Steam games rising rapidly every year, the elephant in the room is AI.

New language models capable of whipping up functioning games very quickly, and Valve's laissez-faire curation, has prompted an explosion of AI slop games on Steam in the past few months—most of which don't receive much visibility, unless you're unlucky enough to browse Steam Next Fest without the "personalized" mode on. A Valve developer recently acknowledged that there's a slop problem on Steam, but said that they don't want to "be gatekeepers telling developers which tools they should or shouldn't use."

If you look at today's update through that lens, perhaps Valve isn't so much speaking to experienced developers with these obvious-sounding guidelines, but vibe coders who lack basic game dev understanding. Also today, Itch.io announced that it deleted "a couple terabytes of AI slop" from its videogame storefront to combat the wave of low-quality games appearing online.

Whatever the cause of the holdup, Valve said more changes are coming to the build review process, but this is a "great starting point."

‘I wasn’t performing’: Bruno Fernandes says Carrick was right to drop him after criticism

📅 2026-11-03  |  Read Source →

‘If it was punishment for the words, it was right’

Carrick hails ‘magical’ Old Trafford after comeback win

Bruno Fernandes said Michael ­Carrick was correct to drop him last month as he reflected on a remarkable Manchester United comeback win against Roma at Old Trafford.

A twice-taken penalty from ­Fernandes set United’s revival from 2-0 down in motion, two goals from Matheus Cunha ultimately sealing a win that could yet save the embattled Carrick’s job.

Fernandes had been dropped after their previous Champions League game, a 4-1 humbling at Como, ­having criticised the manager’s ­tactics in a post-match interview. He was an unused substitute against Bournemouth in their next fixture and said he accepted the short-lived demotion.

“The manager took a decision of making myself on the bench because I wasn’t performing. And that’s fair,” Fernandes said. “Everyone felt like that was a punishment for the words that I had [after Como]. And if it was, it was right.

“The manager makes the ­decisions. I have to accept it. I have to go forward with that. It doesn’t change the belief I have in the manager, the staff and every player at this club. And it doesn’t change my aim to win every game that I have ahead of me.”

Fernandes attempted to take the attention away from Carrick after United’s first win in 10 games, which was sealed amid a raucous atmosphere pockmarked by protests against the club’s ownership. They narrowly avoided completing their longest winless run during a single season since March 1978.

“We don’t need to save anyone’s job,” he said. “The club needed that win. The players needed that win. It is something we needed as a group of players and staff. We have been working so hard to get out of this situation together.”

A satisfied Carrick hailed the magic of Old Trafford after United’s rip-roaring second half. “I don’t like to keep talking to the lads about what this place can do in history and what’s gone before them too much, but it’s there,” he said. “This place does magical things, it’s done it before and it’ll do it over and over again at some point in the future. There’s just something in this place that when we do have these types of night, we do it the hard way.”

He’s got that little glint in his eye, he plays with a bit of mischief if you like.”

OpenAI Wants to Rebrand AI Data Centers, Use Them to 'Protect the Grid'

📅 2026-11-03  |  Read Source →

OpenAI Wants to Rebrand AI Data Centers, Use Them to 'Protect the Grid'

OpenAI wants its massive AI data centers to help keep the lights on when electricity grids come under pressure. And, apparently, it would also prefer that people stop calling them AI data centers.

The ChatGPT developer announced its Grid Protectors Program on Monday, a national initiative designed to let future AI facilities reduce electricity consumption during emergencies and periods of high demand, potentially lowering costs for other customers.

Under the plan, OpenAI would coordinate with utility companies to determine how much electricity its facilities could temporarily stop using, how quickly they could respond, and for how long. The company is also exploring on-site batteries and power generation to help manage demand.

“Families and small businesses should not be asked to subsidize a compute campus’s electricity needs,” OpenAI said in its announcement.

The program addresses one of the biggest complaints surrounding the AI infrastructure boom: companies are building increasingly power-hungry facilities while residents worry about paying higher—and water—electricity bills to support them.

OpenAI is using a planned facility in Effingham County, Georgia, as its first example. The company has signed a 25-year agreement with Georgia Power covering approximately 3,200 megawatts of electricity demand, with up to 1,000 megawatts available for temporary reductions during periods of high demand.

Under the approved agreement, OpenAI must cover the full infrastructure costs required to serve its facility, rather than passing those expenses on to existing customers.

Georgia Power also projects approximately $950 million in annual customer savings beginning in 2029, equivalent to at least $15 per month for a typical residential customer. However, those estimates cover the utility's broader portfolio of large electricity customers and expected growth, not just OpenAI's project.

The Georgia facility is still under development, and the savings remain projections. OpenAI has not yet disclosed binding demand-reduction commitments for other facilities under its national program.

OpenAI's electricity initiative also arrives after reports of a massive PR campaign to change what people call the facilities in the first place to erase from collective memory the negative sentiment towards AI data centers.

According to Axios, OpenAI is preparing a post-midterm policy and PR campaign that includes rebranding data centers as "AI compute campuses," an effort to make the infrastructure sound more appealing to communities, effectively trying to solve a problem by changing the problem’s name.

The company has already started using the terminology. Its Grid Protectors announcement repeatedly refers to future facilities as "compute campuses," although it occasionally uses the more familiar term "data centers."

An OpenAI spokesperson confirmed the naming strategy to Business Insider, arguing that the term better reflects the facilities' broader role in computing, energy infrastructure, and skilled employment.

The reported rebranding comes as opposition to AI infrastructure projects has become a political issue across the United States. Pennsylvania introduced new restrictions on large data centers in August, including provisions designed to protect residents from higher electricity costs. Other states are following.

OpenAI and six other major technology companies also signed a White House pledge in March to pay for the electricity and grid upgrades their AI projects require.

The new program goes further by proposing that AI facilities could become useful to utilities rather than simply adding pressure to their systems. But the national framework is still largely an ambition, with individual agreements to be negotiated.

OpenAI's Georgia project will provide an early test of that approach. Electricity delivery to the planned 3.2-gigawatt facility is scheduled to begin in 2028 and expand in phases through 2032.

"It should be out in the open, clearly labelled, and regulated according to the rules that we can implement on our platform"

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

As AI-assisted development continues to accelerate in fan-driven dev communities, Nexus Mods has outlined an extensive new set of policies on mods created using AI. In short, the site will continue to accept AI mods, but it will soon require modders to disclose their usage of AI-generated content and will allow users to filter out that content entirely if they so choose.

"We are pro-human creativity," Nexus Mods co-owner Foledinho says in a bolded portion of the new announcement. However, the site "won't introduce an outright ban on AI-generated mods. You may ask me why, since that is not the solution some of you were looking for. The answer is simple: AI is currently used throughout the whole process of creation, from script autocompletion to generation of textures, voices, and full assets, and it is difficult to draw a clear boundary between what is acceptable and unacceptable."

Foledinho argues that "an outright ban will not eliminate AI from the modding process but will prevent people from mentioning its use. We'd prefer the opposite; it should be out in the open, clearly labelled, and regulated according to the rules that we can implement on our platform."

To that end, Nexus Mods is "making AI disclosure mandatory when uploading a new mod, meaning Mod Authors will need to declare whether AI has been used in their work." An example screenshot suggests that modders will be able to declare whether their mod is primarily AI-generated, simply assisted by AI, or if it features AI-generated promotional material. Modders will also be required to give a specific "public explanation of how and where AI was used."

It all sounds fairly similar to the AI disclosures currently in use on Steam – nobody tell Tim Sweeney – but there's a key difference in how Nexus Mods will handle all this: users will be able to filter AI-generated content out of their searches entirely. "Some of you would prefer not to see any AI-generated content, and that’s your decision to make," Foledinho says.

Existing mods will have "a 60-day grace period" to meet the new requirements, which will run from November 3 to January 2, 2027. Once the grace period is over, "we will start directly enforcing these rules. Authors will be notified and asked to fix missing disclosures or permission settings." If there's "continued failure" to follow the disclosure rules, moderators will step in, potentially restricting that user's account or removing them from Nexus Mods entirely.

"While we hope these changes will encourage everyone to disclose their AI usage, to be honest, that’s not going to happen," Foledinho admits, but the site devs hope that having "clear moderation standards in place" will encourage modders to be up front about how they're using AI.

Eney from MacPaw is an AI assistant with deep Apple integrations

MacPaw, the makers of CleanMyMac and Setapp, have an AI assistant called Eney that’s designed for Apple users. Eney is available across Mac and iPhone and is built to integrate with core Apple services like Mail, Calendar, and Reminders, plus a lot more.

There are a lot of AI tools out there, but not many from teams who have a long history of serving and understanding the needs of Apple users.

That’s one way Eney, the AI assistant from MacPaw, stands out.

MacPaw is the company behind CleanMyMac and Setapp, two popular tools among Apple users. And Eney is its Apple-native take on an AI assistant.

Eney is a proactive assistant on both Mac and iPhone that takes tedious busywork off users’ plates. It can help out with summarizing documents, drafting emails, organizing files, planning your day, and a lot more. A few standout features include:

Connectors are another big tentpole feature. They enable Eney to integrate directly with services like Apple Mail, Apple Calendar, and Reminders, as well as Google Drive, Docs, and Calendar.

Privacy is always a key issue with any AI tool, and MacPaw has built several privacy-sensitive protections into Eney. prince de conti hotel turns historic parisian interiors into theatrical guest rooms

📅 2026-11-03  |  Read Source →

set in saint-germain-des-prés, the 23-room boutique retreat blends rich tapestries, sumptuous fabrics and historical references with contemporary interiors inspired by 17th-century french elegance.

The Prince de Conti hotel reimagines a 17th-century Parisian building, infusing it with contemporary interior design within the historic Saint-Germain-des-Prés district. With just 23 rooms and suites, the four-star hotel has little interest in playing by the usual rules. Instead, it borrows the extravagance of its five-star counterparts, packing rich textures, theatrical details, and a healthy dose of 17th-century drama into a surprisingly intimate setting. Think less conventional hotel, more Parisian jewel box with a flair for the dramatic. Its prime location places it steps away from iconic landmarks such as Notre-Dame de Paris and the Hôtel de la Monnaie.

The Paristory group, a family-managed enterprise led by sisters Alexandra and Julie Marang, owns the Prince de Conti. This hotel marks a significant expansion for the group, as it is their first property situated on the Rive Gauche, a neighborhood holding personal significance for the Marang family. The group already operates three successful boutique hotels on the Rive Droite, bringing their expertise in hospitality and refined design to this new venture in the heart of Paris.

The interior design concept at the Prince de Conti draws extensively on historical references, which are carefully reinterpreted through a contemporary lens. The result is a playful mix of bold colors, historical motifs, and carefully chosen details, where the 17th century gets a contemporary makeover. Rather than recreating the past, the interiors borrow its most theatrical elements and give them a new role, creating spaces that feel somewhere between a private residence and a period drama, minus the powdered wigs.

The 17th century makes its presence felt throughout, with nods to the larger-than-life characters and events of the period. Rich wood finishes, elaborate tapestries, and sumptuous fabrics set the scene, borrowing a few tricks from the French aristocracy’s decorating playbook. Historical references are given a contemporary twist, bringing a touch of old-world extravagance into the present without turning the hotel into a museum.

No two rooms tell quite the same story. Each of the 23 rooms and suites is treated as a miniature stage set, with its own character, decorative details, and historical references. Theatrical touches abound, but the scale remains intimate, making the experience feel more like stepping into an eccentric private residence than checking into a conventional hotel.

That sense of informality extends to the hotel’s honesty bar, where guests can help themselves to wine, spirits, or other drinks and simply keep track of what they’ve had. No bartender, no elaborate rituals, just a little old-fashioned trust. It’s a refreshingly relaxed touch in a setting that otherwise takes its decorative drama rather seriously.

name: Prince de Conti | @hotelprincedeconti location: Paris, France

Please stop asking me when the AI bubble will pop

📅 2026-11-03  |  Read Source →

Posts from this topic will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Posts from this topic will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

I’m getting some real mixed messages here, boys!

Posts from this author will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Posts from this author will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

People, including on this staff, keep asking me with varying degrees of excitement or trepidation when the AI bubble will burst. I’ll tell you what I tell them: If I knew that, I would make a life-changing bet on the market, levered up as far as I could, and then, after I made my money, go retire on a beach somewhere. Maybe run a cat sanctuary, who knows.

What I can tell you is that I read with interest a story in Bloomberg this morning: “Wall Street CEOs, Big Money Managers Divided Over AI Risks.” The story itself is a roundup of stuff said at the 2026 Global Financial Leaders’ Investment Summit in Hong Kong. Here’s what caught my attention: The CEO of Pacific Investment Management Co. — that’s PIMCO, the legendary bond firm — told listeners that there’s going to be a downturn in the AI market “and then things are going to break.”

“What we don’t know is how pricing and economics will evolve.”

The astronomically expensive AI buildout has swamped debt markets — making it harder to raise cash. But there’s still more debt coming, and people still want to buy it. For instance, Meta drew $58 billion in orders for a bond offering that’s expected to be $15 billion to $20 billion, Bloomberg reported today. Oracle said Monday it would need another $20 billion in bonds, on top of its existing steep commitments. Alphabet, Google’s parent company, is selling €6 billion (or about $6.7 billion) of bonds. Even Microsoft recently sold bonds, for the first time in a decade — $25 billion of them.

The PIMCO CEO — his name is Emmanuel Roman, but his job is what matters here — has estimated that the AI buildout could create $2 trillion in financing needs over the next five years. The entire total US bond market is about $12 trillion.

I am harping on PIMCO because it has been instrumental in the AI trade. It was among the group of financiers that did the first CoreWeave chip-based financing in 2023. It made a quick $2 billion in paper gains from $29 billion in Meta bonds, meant to support the AI buildout, last year. It bought $4 billion of SpaceX bonds last month. If PIMCO’s CEO is publicly sounding the alarm about a bubble popping, that’s a big deal — especially since the company just sold a bunch of its AI debt.

It’s not just PIMCO urging caution. Apollo Global Management, which lately had been talking with SpaceX about a $40 billion chip deal, is also warning of AI risk. Apollo president Jim Zelter told analysts today that while he expects society will use “dramatically more computing power in the future” and that AI is improving quickly, there is still significant uncertainty. “What we don’t know is how pricing and economics will evolve.”

The sheer volume of interest on Meta’s bonds suggests we are not at the top of the market. But there have been reports from China that its AI bubble burst some time ago. Meanwhile Wall Street banks were tripping over themselves to finance $63 billion in debt from Anthropic — not even a hyperscaler.

This is what I mean when I say “I don’t know.” I do think, though, that something major — PIMCO’s Roman singled out US rate hikes, war, or a eurozone crisis as potential triggers — could flip this market. We’re not in go-go-go territory now. Maybe that will change; it’s possible Anthropic’s IPO will go blockbuster and it’ll be like when someone rolls up to a party that’s running low on beer with a 24-pack. But as more people are expressing caution — and particularly after the disaster at Situational Awareness — the best part of the party might be over.

Gasperini analyzes loss to Man United: “It teaches us a lot.”

📅 2026-11-03  |  Read Source →

A terrible late defeat condemned Roma to the Champions League.

Leading the way, the Giallorossi saw Manchester United overturn their lead in the final minutes: first a penalty from Bruno Fernandes, then a brace from Matheus Cunha.

The Brazilian first leveled the scoreline with a mistake from Balerdi, then scored the winner in the 86th minute, capping his wild night with a red card in the 91st.

After the match, Gian Piero Gasperini analyzed the painful defeat to Sky Sport, starting with the episode that changed the match’s momentum: “I think it was a different game than the one against Real Madrid,” the Giallorossi coach explained. “There weren’t such obvious signs of a decline, as there were after the penalty, which gave them a great boost.”

No excuses, however, for the inability to stem the Red Devils’ final assault: “There’s no coincidence in the goals we conceded in the comeback; tonight we didn’t have the strength to defend their attackers. It’s natural that you can struggle against Manchester United in the final 15 minutes if they attack like that.”

“They started to press further forward; we had to play differently, taking advantage of long balls. We weren’t able to keep up with them: we should have resorted to a few more tackles, but instead we suffered their momentum, which was amplified by the penalty and the 2-2 incident.”

On managing the right flank and using Lulli in place of the injured Rensch (with Molina left out): “Molina could have stayed in, yes. Rensch started well, then he got injured and I opted for Lulli. But that’s not the reason for the defeat. I think it was a different match, which taught us more than the one against Real Madrid.” He concluded by focusing on the psychological aspect of dealing with the backlash: “There was a bit of disappointment after seeing victory slip away from you at the front. The team perhaps needs to relieve itself of the pressure of a result at all costs.”

Grant Kirkhope Of DK Rap Fame Wants To Soundtrack Assassin's Creed

📅 2026-11-03  |  Read Source →

Sam Woods is TheGamer’s Senior Reporter. He’s been in games journalism since 2020 and has undertaken a variety of roles in that time. These have included roles such as SEO Editor, Managing Editor at DualShockers, and Global Head of Gaming, and he's also been seen on the BBC. He was promoted to the Senior Reporter position in 2025 following a series of high-quality reports and exclusives for TheGamer.

Since 2020, Sam has written over 3,000 articles and specializes in telling the stories of the people behind the games. He’s interviewed the biggest game directors, some of the industry’s top talents, told the stories of how indie games came to be, and spoken to players about what really makes them tick. He’s also interviewed some of the world’s biggest musical artists to find out how the medium has inspired their work, and TV and Film actors about their roles in video game adaptations. He even hosted a PAX panel in 2021 and made a documentary in 2024 surrounding the creation of a Disney game.

Alongside this, Sam focuses on breaking news. He got the scoop on Perfect Dark’s cancellation, provided context on some AI drama, and revealed that Lady Dimitrescu’s actress would love to step back into the character’s giant shoes. These articles have been picked up by the biggest gaming sites, including IGN, GameSpot, and PC Gamer. He also loves to dive into the numbers behind the games.

Sam’s journey in games journalism has taken him all over the world. He’s covered Gamescom in Germany, Summer Game Fest in Los Angeles, and Tokyo Game Show in Japan, each on three occasions.

He’s been learning Japanese since 2024 with the intention of identifying and translating Japanese-language news and bringing it to English-language audiences first, something he’s done countless times.

Sam’s first review was 2020’s Battletoads reboot, and he’s since reviewed games in The Legend of Zelda, Super Mario, and Pokemon franchises.

Grant Kirkhope has had an illustrious career. In addition to winning an Ivor Novello Award for his work on Mario + Rabbids: Sparks of Hope, the composer has created music for games like Donkey Kong 64, Banjo-Kazooie, Viva Piñata, Street Fighter 6, World of Warcraft, and Sid Meier's Civilization.

His whimsical style suits more cartoony games, and he's a big reason for Rare's popularity in the late 90s and early 2000s, but he's a serious composer. Having worked with Ubisoft a few times over recent years, I asked Kirkhope if there was any series from the developer he'd like to tackle, and his answer surprised me, saying an Assassin's Creed game would be "a really cool project to work on".

Grant Kirkhope and Ubisoft are forming a strong relationship. In 2017, he created the soundtrack for Mario + Rabbids: Kingdom Battle, and a few years later, he did the same for its sequel, Sparks of Hope—including its Rayman-focused DLC, Rayman in the Phantom Shadow. This ultimately led to him getting the gig creating the music for Rayman Legends Retold's Night of the Livid Dead level, which we interviewed him about (and you can read it right here).

I asked Kirkhope if there was any other Ubisoft IP he'd like to work on, and Assassin's Creed sprang to mind.

“I'd love to have a crack at that, I've got to say. That would be a really cool project to work on, because it's such a cool game. And it's been through so many iterations of different scenarios,” he says. “London, Japan, the big massive Viking one. I’d love to do it. I think it’s unlikely, but I’d love to."

With such an interesting answer, I asked the composer if there were a time period and location for an Assassin's Creed game he'd be particularly keen to work on.

“Since I’m Scottish, it would have to be the whole Scotland-against-England thing. Back in the Middle Ages. William Wallace. Robert the Bruce. I could be there. I could do that. Get me my bagpipes,” he tells me.

While it's clear he's enjoying working on Ubisoft titles, Kirkhope admitted he wouldn't be working on Beyond Good and Evil. Despite saying it looked "super cool", when I posed the question to him, the answer was a resounding "no."

Fractal Analytics Profit Climbs 139% as Healthcare Leads Growth

📅 2026-11-03  |  Read Source →

Fractal Analytics crossed $100 million in quarterly revenue for the first time and lifted net income 139% to INR 792 million in its fiscal second quarter, according to results released on November 3, 2026. Revenue from operations rose 20% year over year to INR 9.565 billion.

The Q2 financial handbook separates the headline earnings measure from Fractal’s adjusted one. EBITDA rose 71.4% to INR 1.557 billion, while adjusted EBITDA increased 29.6% to INR 1.549 billion. The company’s adjusted version starts with EBITDA and adjusts for items including employee stock-option expense, other income and its share of an associate’s loss. Adjusted EBITDA margin was 16.2%, compared with 15.0% a year earlier, while profit-after-tax margin reached 8.3%, up from 4.2%.

Finance costs fell to INR 53 million from INR 115 million, while depreciation and amortization rose to INR 472 million from INR 335 million. Lower finance costs helped the bottom line, while higher depreciation and amortization partly offset that benefit, adding context to the sharp net-income increase.

Healthcare and life sciences revenue grew 79% year over year and represented 25.1% of the quarterly industry mix. Banking, financial services and insurance grew 30% and accounted for 13.6%. Consumer packaged goods and retail remained the largest vertical at 34.7% of revenue and grew 11%, while TMT grew 6% sequentially but fell 18% year over year.

In his shareholder letter, co-founder and Group CEO Srikanth Velamakanni said the group grew 33% in rupee terms excluding TMT and described 10% constant-currency growth as below the company’s ambition.

He mainly attributed the 225-basis-point gross-margin decline, to 44.0%, to investing ahead of growth in AI engineering talent. Separately, research and development investment was INR 397 million, or 6.3% of revenue.

Fractal’s products, including its flagship Cogentiq agentic AI platform, reached an annual revenue run-rate of INR 1.53 billion, up 39% year over year, according to the shareholder letter.

The core Fractal.ai segment generated INR 9.361 billion of revenue, up 20.7%. Its adjusted segment-results margin was 17.7%, compared with 17.5% a year earlier; Fractal calculates the underlying measure by adding employee stock-option costs, including cash bonuses, and acquisition-retention bonuses back to segment results.

Net revenue retention in Fractal.ai, which tracks spending from an existing client cohort including expansions and contractions, improved to 115.2% from 112.2%. Six clients contributed more than $20 million annually, compared with four a year earlier.

Fractal Alpha revenue grew 39.9% to INR 340 million. Its adjusted segment loss widened to INR 104 million from INR 84 million, but the adjusted margin improved to negative 30.6% from negative 34.6%, leaving the smaller business growing alongside continued losses.

For the six months ended September 30, 2026, revenue reached INR 18.69 billion, up 20%, and net income reached INR 1.515 billion, up 114%. Velamakanni described the first-half revenue growth as entirely organic. Quarterly cash flow from operations was INR 793 million after an INR 1.03 billion outflow in Q1 FY27.

The unaudited financial statements recorded INR 237 million of net cash used in operating activities for the half, compared with INR 214 million a year earlier. Trade receivables absorbed INR 1.4 billion of working capital during the period, helping explain why higher accounting profit coexisted with negative operating cash flow.

Fractal ended the period with INR 17.28 billion in cash and investments and no long-term debt, according to Velamakanni’s letter. The balance sheet listed no current borrowings, compared with INR 2.86 billion at March 31, 2026.

The results leave a clear operating tension: healthcare, products and the roster of large clients are expanding, while TMT is contracting, gross margin is under pressure and first-half operating cash flow remains negative. The central issue is whether Fractal can convert its faster profit growth into cash while sustaining investment in AI engineering and products.

Give Scrambled Eggs An Instant Flavor Boost With A Packet You Have In The Pantry

📅 2026-11-03  |  Read Source →

We may receive a commission on purchases made from links.

When you want an easy breakfast, eggs are key to a protein-rich morning meal that can be made in minutes — but for maximum flavor, you might want to think about scrambling them because you can mix them with any spice, herb, or seasoning you'd like. For example, pairing taco seasoning with your scrambled eggs creates an incomparable taste that requires only a few seconds of extra work.

For the simplest preparation, you can just whisk a teaspoon of the seasoning — we like McCormick Original Taco Seasoning Mix, available on Amazon — to about six eggs before you cook them, which will feed two to three people for breakfast. Most premade taco seasoning mixes contain plenty of salt, plus cumin, garlic powder, onion powder, and other spices, so you may be ready to go right out of the packet without needing to add anything else. But you can customize it to get exactly the flavor profile you're looking for.

If you're eating the eggs right off the plate, think about adding some cheese — Monterey Jack melts beautifully, and you can add a little extra spice with pepper jack. But cheddar is always a good choice and can add a sharper flavor, depending on what you use. For more creaminess, try Havarti, or go for Comté for some nutty undertones. From there, some freshly chopped cilantro or a drizzle of hot sauce makes for delicious toppings.

If you want to make a whole meal, you can add some extra ingredients to your plate. Putting a little chorizo in your scrambled eggs will give them a richer, spicier flavor, plus more protein. Or think about making summery breakfast tacos or burritos. You can cook sauteed onions, potatoes, and bell peppers, then mix it all together with your spiced eggs. All you need to do is wrap the mixture in your favorite tortilla, and you can enjoy it at the breakfast table or on the go.

Ahmed Barakat is a journalist and copywriter based in Georgia with a growing focus on blockchain technology, DeFi, AI, privacy, digital assets, and fintech innovation.

The CryptoNews editorial team is composed of seasoned writers specializing in cryptocurrency and blockchain technology. Their expertise ensures comprehensive, accurate, and insightful content for...

Sam Altman’s ChatGPT AI predicts ‘Will crypto recover?’. When prompted, the OpenAI bot gave an in-depth answer on whether and when crypto will see brighter days.

It believes that although the market has pulled back from its previous cycle highs and remains in a consolidation phase, there is a chance of a full-blown bull market returning.

It argues that Bitcoin could print a new all-time high and remain viable over the coming months if liquidity conditions improve and risk appetite returns.

Bitcoin is currently trading in the $76,800–$77,400 range after testing lower supports near $76,000. On the higher timeframes, price continues to hold above key intermediate demand zones while facing resistance around $80,000–$82,000 and then the $85,000–$90,000 area.

A sustained breakout and hold above $90,000–$95,000 would strengthen the case for a larger recovery structure targeting a retest of the prior all-time high near $124,000–$126,000, with measured-move extensions potentially carrying further.

Momentum indicators sit in neutral-to-oversold territory on intermediate timeframes, leaving room for a stronger upside impulse. Ethereum is consolidating in the $2,250–$2,300 region.

It has held key support near $2,200–$2,250 while facing resistance around $2,400–$2,500 and then $2,700–$2,800. A decisive reclaim of the $2,800–$3,000 zone would improve the intermediate trend and open the path toward a recovery of the prior cycle high near $4,900.

Relative strength versus Bitcoin remains a key watchpoint; improving ETH/BTC performance would typically accompany broader risk-on conditions.

Several external factors could improve the chances of recovery. Globally, a durable resolution or meaningful de-escalation in major geopolitical conflicts, such as the Russia-Ukraine war or a lasting ceasefire involving Iran that normalizes shipping through the Strait of Hormuz, would reduce risk premiums and support broader risk assets.

Economically, clearer signals of monetary easing or a sustained period of elevated liquidity from major central banks would benefit high-beta assets like cryptocurrencies.

Within the crypto ecosystem itself, continued institutional adoption (including ETF flows, corporate treasury allocations, and advances in tokenization), regulatory clarity in major jurisdictions, and a renewed capital rotation from Bitcoin into higher-beta altcoins would all contribute to a more sustained bull market phase.
